# Rights, sources and translation policy

Verso cites the source and an explicit rights note on each poem and translation. These notes are editorial guidance, **not legal advice**; public-domain terms and moral rights differ by jurisdiction. Do not assume that a public-domain original makes a later translation, scholarly edition or transcription free to reuse.

## Launch sources

- Five individually documented Ukrainian poems add **Lesya Ukrainka**'s *Досвітні огні*, *Як дитиною бувало* and *Слово, чому ти не твердая криця*, and **Ivan Franko**'s *Каменярі* and *Гимн*. Each complete poem is counted once, not again for a different printing, translation, quotation or musical setting. The educational and critical sources below document attention to individual works, not global rankings or permission to copy modern commentary.
  - *Досвітні огні* has seven five-line stanzas in [*На крилах пісень*, title page 1892, pp. 25–26](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Леся_Українка._На_крилах_пісень._1892.pdf?page=25). Publication histories also give 1893. The [collected-edition notes](https://www.l-ukrainka.name/uk/Verses/NaKrylachPisen/DosvitniVogni.html) say the author omitted two stanzas in the 1904 edition. The first-book reading prints `стяга та просвітла`; the later reading quoted there has `стяга та пресвітла`. [Franko's 1898 essay](https://uk.wikisource.org/wiki/Леся_Українка_(Франко)) names the individual poem, criticizes its overall execution and praises a stanza; this is not unqualified praise. [Освіта.UA](https://osvita.ua/school/literature/u/65799/) also presents the poem separately. The specialist page reported 99,226 loads on 17 November 2026, not necessarily unique readers.
  - *Як дитиною бувало* comprises four quatrains on [p. 93 / PDF p. 96 of *Думи і мрії* (1899)](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Леся_Українка_–_Думи_і_мрії._Поезиї_(1899).pdf?page=96), between printed separators; the opening supplies its title. A [sixth-grade textbook](https://uahistory.co/pidruchniki/avramenko-ukraine-literature-6-class-2014/10.php) prints it separately; [another](https://uahistory.co/pidruchniki/kalynych-ukraine-literature-6-class-2023/26.php) names it in a poetry selection.
  - *Слово, чому ти не твердая криця* has six quatrains on [pp. 81–82 / PDF pp. 84–85](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Леся_Українка_–_Думи_і_мрії._Поезиї_(1899).pdf?page=84). Its [earlier 1897 periodical printing](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Житє_і_слово,_вісник_літератури,_історіі_і_фольклору._Видає_Ольга_Франко._Том_VI_(1897).pdf?page=19) is individually listed in the volume's contents and reads `справжнім мечем`, while the selected 1899 printing reads `кращим мечем`. The scan reads `мого серця`; the credited transcription of p. 82 has `мого сєрця`, corrected here. A [tenth-grade textbook](https://uahistory.co/pidruchniki/fasolya-ukraine-literature-10-class-2018-standard-level/45.php) discusses the poem individually.
  - *Каменярі* is separately headed and complete in 55 lines in [*Украінська Муза* (1908), issue 4, columns 413–414 / PDF p. 41](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Украінська_Муза_(Випуск_4).pdf?page=41). The anthology prints the poem continuously; wrapped lines are rejoined. It reads `роспуски`, possibly a printing error; the [1893 collection](https://uk.wikisource.org/wiki/З_вершин_і_низин_(1893)/Каменярі) reads `розпуки`. The scan's `сердиті` corrects the credited transcription's `сердити`. [Collected-edition notes](https://i-franko.name/uk/verses/zvershyninyzyn/excelsior/kamenjari.html) identify the independent 1878 publication and quote Franko saying the allegory specifies neither time nor place. A [tenth-grade textbook](https://uahistory.co/pidruchniki/paharenko-ukraine-literature-10-class-2018-profile-level/25.php) analyzes the individual poem.
  - *Гимн* is four ten-line stanzas in [*З вершин і низин* (1893), pp. 9–10 / PDF pp. 13–14](https://archive.org/download/zvershyninyzynzb00fran/zvershyninyzynzb00fran.pdf#page=13). Its subtitle is `Замісць пролога`, but [collected-edition notes](https://i-franko.name/uk/verses/zvershyninyzyn/gimn.html) identify its separately titled 1882 publication before its placement as a prologue. The 1893 printing has `думка воля` without a comma. A [1908 anthology printing](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Украінська_Муза_(Випуск_4).pdf?page=43) stops after three stanzas; it is neither the source of this reading nor a second work. A [tenth-grade textbook](https://uahistory.co/pidruchniki/fasolya-ukraine-literature-10-class-2018-standard-level/14.php) analyzes the poem.
  - The historical printings are public domain in the United States; Ukrainka died in 1913 and Franko in 1916. Copyright, edition-related and moral rights vary by jurisdiction; check local law. The readings adapt scan-checked Ukrainian Wikisource contributor transcriptions under [CC BY-SA 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/); each reader credits and links all pinned revisions. Historical wording and printed stress marks are retained, while indentation, spacing and some punctuation are normalized; printings are not silently spliced. Scans are linked, not republished; no translation or recording is included. Ukraine is an associated place, not a claim about composition; no religious category is inferred from author identity. `python3 scripts/import-recognized-ukrainian-classics-2.py --check` reproduces the [readings](../scripts/sources/recognized-ukrainian-classics-2-readings.txt); optional `--evidence-dir <directory>` checks the four consulted source-copy hashes. Hashes identify copies, not transcription accuracy; duplicate screens cannot exclude every translation or variant.

- Two individually documented Dutch-language poems follow historical printings: **Willem Kloos**, *Ik ween om bloemen, in den knop gebroken*, and **Frederik van Eeden**, *De waterlelie*. The [1997 review of a Dutch teachers' survey](https://www.dbnl.org/tekst/_tsj001199701_01/_tsj001199701_01_0028.php) names each work in a chronological selection, documenting educational attention rather than a global ranking. Each complete work is counted once, not again for another reading, translation or musical setting; the Netherlands is an associated place, not a composition-location claim.
  - Kloos's fourteen-line sonnet is numbered LXI on [p. 73, PDF p. 79 of *Verzen I*, second printing, Amsterdam, 1902](https://archive.org/download/verzen00kloogoog/verzen00kloogoog.pdf#page=79); adjacent pages contain sonnets LX and LXII. The printed heading is a number, so the opening supplies the catalogue title. [A publisher](https://vantilt.nl/boeken/verzen/) identifies the poem in the earlier 1894 volume; the era reflects that evidence, while the recorded year identifies the 1902 reading. Our 1902 page reads `heen-gegaan`, `ééns`, `éer` and `geblaêrte`; a [teaching comparison](https://www.rozemarijnonline.net/gastboek/vraag201920.html), citing 1894, prints `heengegaan`, `ééns`, `eer` and `geblaarte`. We do not silently combine the readings. Kloos died in 1938.
  - Van Eeden's eight-line *De waterlelie* is the first poem in [*Enkele verzen*, Amsterdam, 1898, unnumbered page, PDF p. 11](https://archive.org/download/enkeleverzen00eedegoog/enkeleverzen00eedegoog.pdf#page=11); the next page begins *De Lente*. The title page gives the 1898 imprint and the [Archive record](https://archive.org/details/enkeleverzen00eedegoog) identifies the author; [the University of Pennsylvania catalogue](https://onlinebooks.library.upenn.edu/webbin/who/Eeden%2C%20Frederik%20van%2C%201860-1932) independently lists this edition. Its `water-lelie`, `zoo` and `wenscht` are preserved; a [teaching comparison](https://www.rozemarijnonline.net/poezie/eeden-waterlelie.html), also citing 1898, modernizes `zoo` and `wenscht`. Van Eeden died in 1932. Later appearances or settings do not make additional works.
  - Both readings were transcribed from the linked historical page images; spacing, indentation and apostrophe typography are normalized. The historical printings are public domain in the United States. Copyright, edition-related and moral rights vary elsewhere; check local law. The facsimiles and later commentary are linked, not republished; no translations, illustrations, recordings or religious classifications inferred from the authors are included. `python3 scripts/import-recognized-dutch-classics-2.py --check` reproduces the [reading snapshot](../scripts/sources/recognized-dutch-classics-2-readings.txt); optional `--evidence-dir <directory>` verifies both consulted PDF hashes. Hashes identify the copies, not transcription accuracy; duplicate screens cannot exclude every translation or paraphrase.

- Three individually documented poems by **Dan Andersson**, *Spelmannen*, *Minnet* and *Jag har drömt…*, follow the [1921 *Visor och ballader*](https://runeberg.org/danvob/), which includes *Svarta ballader*. Each complete poem is counted once, not again for variants, quotations or musical settings. Sweden is an associated place, not a composition-location claim; no translations, recordings or religious classifications inferred from the author are added.
  - *Spelmannen* has six quatrains on [pp. 87–88](https://runeberg.org/danvob/0089.html), after the separately headed *En spelmans jordafärd*. [Litteraturbanken](https://litteraturbanken.se/skolan/poesi-forfattare-dan-andersson-spelmannen/) teaches the individual poem; [Ludvika's memorial project](https://dananderssonveckan.com/minnesstenar/spelmannen/) also selects it. The commemorative page's recording and its claim of a 1915 collection date are not used for this reading.
  - *Minnet*, beginning `Vi gingo över Raiskis`, has 18 complete lines on [pp. 115–116](https://runeberg.org/danvob/0117.html). Its second eight-line block crosses the page turn; a smaller printed gap precedes the final two lines. This presentation preserves the final gap but does not insert a stanza break at the page turn. The poem is distinct from Andersson's [posthumous same-titled poem](https://svenskadikter.com/Minnet_(Efterl%C3%A4mnade_dikter)), which begins `Det stod en natt med stjärnor`. [Litteraturbanken](https://litteraturbanken.se/skolan/poesi-forfattare-dan-andersson-minnet/) teaches the Raiskis poem; [Ludvika's memorial project](https://dananderssonveckan.com/minnesstenar/) quotes its ending.
  - *Jag har drömt…* has eight quatrains on [pp. 156–157](https://runeberg.org/danvob/0158.html), before *Vaggsången vid Kestina*. The 1921 printing reads `en liten, liten kvinna`; [Litteraturbanken's teaching text](https://litteraturbanken.se/skolan/poesi-forfattare-dan-andersson-jag-har-dromt/) omits that comma. The Royal Swedish Academy of Music's [catalogue](https://www.levandemusikarv.se/tonsattare/rangstrom-ture/SMH-W7391-Vildmark_Tva_ballader_av_Dan_Andersson) identifies a 1924 setting of the poem; the setting is not reproduced or counted separately.
  - The school selections and memorial or music-catalogue evidence document individual regional attention, not global rankings or permission to copy modern commentary, recordings or settings. The readings were checked against the page images and teaching texts; the printing's wording is retained rather than silently combined with later readings. Printer-wrapped lines are rejoined; heading ellipses, spacing, indentation and dash typography are normalized. The scanned [title page](https://runeberg.org/danvob/0005.html) identifies the 1921 printing, not necessarily the composition dates.
  - The 1921 printing is public domain in the United States; Andersson died in 1920. Copyright, edition-related and moral rights vary elsewhere; check local law. The facsimile is linked, not republished. `python3 scripts/import-recognized-swedish-classics-2.py --check` reproduces the [reading snapshot](../scripts/sources/recognized-swedish-classics-2-readings.txt); optional `--evidence-dir <directory>` verifies the title, contents and six poem-page hashes. Hashes identify the consulted copies, not transcription accuracy; duplicate screens cannot exclude every translation or paraphrase.

- Two individually documented poems by **C. P. Cavafy**, *Η πόλις* and *Περιμένοντας τους βαρβάρους*, follow separate early *Νέα Ζωή* printings. [Words Without Borders](https://wordswithoutborders.org/read/article/2005-04/the-city-the-spirit-and-the-letter-on-translating-cavafy/) discusses translations of *The City*; [The New Yorker](https://www.newyorker.com/books/page-turner/waiting-for-the-barbarians-and-the-government-shutdown) discusses *Waiting for the Barbarians* individually. These document attention to the works, not a global ranking or permission to reproduce modern commentary or translations. Each poem is counted once, not once per edition, translation, quotation or setting.
  - *Η πόλις* has two eight-line stanzas on [p. 129](https://kosmopolis.library.upatras.gr/index.php/nea_zoi/article/view/64853). This printing reads `Καινούριους`, `θἄβρεις` and `τὴν ζωή`; the [pinned Wikisource transcription](https://el.wikisource.org/w/index.php?title=%CE%97_%CF%80%CF%8C%CE%BB%CE%B9%CF%82&oldid=91350) reads `Καινούργιους`, `θάβρεις` and `τὴ ζωή`. A faint mark after `αὐτὰ` is not treated as a certain comma. The scan shows volume 6, issue 4; the Patras catalogue assigns it to 1910, while the [Cavafy bibliography](https://kavafis.greeklanguage.gr/index.php/anazitisi?stext=%CE%97%20%CF%80%CF%8C%CE%BB%CE%B9%CF%82) gives 1909–1910. The exact year is left unset rather than asserted as a composition date.
  - *Περιμένοντας τους βαρβάρους* is a 36-line dialogue on [pp. 98–99](https://kosmopolis.library.upatras.gr/index.php/nea_zoi/article/view/64747); the six-line question about the officials crosses the page turn after `ἀμεθύστους`. The printing reads `συνασθροισμένοι`, `σκαλιγμένα`, `σύγχισις` and `κ’ ἡ πλατέες`, which may include printing errors; the penultimate line ends with a full stop. These are retained and disclosed, with the more familiar opening spelling supplied only as a search alias. The orators' reply also differs from the [pinned modern Wikisource transcription](https://el.wikisource.org/w/index.php?title=%CE%A0%CE%B5%CF%81%CE%B9%CE%BC%CE%AD%CE%BD%CE%BF%CE%BD%CF%84%CE%B1%CF%82_%CF%84%CE%BF%CF%85%CF%82_%CE%B2%CE%B1%CF%81%CE%B2%CE%AC%CF%81%CE%BF%CF%85%CF%82&oldid=123405). The [shorter *Εθνικόν Ημερολόγιον* printing labelled 1907, p. 128](https://kosmopolis.library.upatras.gr/index.php/hmerologio_skokou/article/view/74437), is a comparison, not a second work or source of inserted lines; the [Cavafy bibliography](https://kavafis.greeklanguage.gr/index.php/anazitisi?stext=%CE%A0%CE%B5%CF%81%CE%B9%CE%BC%CE%AD%CE%BD%CE%BF%CE%BD%CF%84%CE%B1%CF%82%20%CF%84%CE%BF%CF%85%CF%82%20%CE%B2%CE%B1%CF%81%CE%B2%CE%AC%CF%81%CE%BF%CF%85%CF%82) dates that almanac's issue to 1906. It cites the *Νέα Ζωή* printing as issue 51, December 1908, while Patras labels it 51–52.
  - The early original printings are public domain in the United States; Cavafy died in 1933. Copyright, edition-related and moral rights vary by jurisdiction; check local law. The readings adapt Greek Wikisource revisions begun by **Origenis** and **Dada**, respectively, and edited by contributors, under [CC BY-SA 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/); they were modified and compared with the University of Patras Kosmopolis digitizations, which Patras likewise offers under CC BY-SA 4.0. Scans, translations and recordings are linked or discussed, not republished. Indentation, spacing and some punctuation and typography are normalized; variants from separate printings are not silently combined. Egypt is an associated place, not a claim of composition there. `python3 scripts/import-recognized-greek-classics-3.py --check` reproduces the [snapshot](../scripts/sources/recognized-greek-classics-3-readings.txt); optional `--evidence-dir <directory>` checks the two source PDFs and the comparison PDF named in the importer. Hashes identify consulted copies, not transcription accuracy; duplicate screens cannot rule out every translation or paraphrase.

- Two individually documented poems by **C. P. Cavafy**, *Τρώες* and *Το πρώτο σκαλί*, follow the January 1927 *Alexandriní Téchnē*, issue 2. A [Lund University MA thesis](https://lup.lub.lu.se/student-papers/record/4359487) discusses *Τρώες* individually and calls it among the poet's better-known works; the [Greek digital schoolbook](https://ebooks.edu.gr/ebooks/v/html/8547/2700/Keimena-Neoellinikis-Logotechnias_A-Lykeiou_html-empl/extras/texts/IndexD3_parallilokeimeno1.html) selects *Το πρώτο σκαλί*. These establish individual educational or literary attention, not global rankings, and do not grant permission to reuse modern translations or commentary.
  - *Τρώες* occupies [p. 2](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Αλεξανδρινή_Τέχνη_Τομ.1_Αρ.2_-_02.jpg); its sections Α΄ and Β΄ form **one poem**, not two additions. The 1927 printing begins `Εἶν’ ἡ προσπάθειές μας σὰν τῶν Τρώων` and lacks the additional opening line in the modern edition quoted in the Lund thesis. An independently digitized [1908 *Εθνικόν Ημερολόγιον* printing, p. 316](https://kosmopolis.library.upatras.gr/index.php/hmerologio_skokou/article/view/72961), likewise begins with the Trojan comparison and has two numbered parts, although its wording differs; its digital copy is offered by the University of Patras under CC BY-SA 4.0. This comparison does not establish why the versions differ or whether the omitted line was in earlier printings. The last line appears to end in a comma in the 1927 periodical, retained here; a separately headed poem starts on p. 3. The adaptation corrects the [pinned Greek Wikisource transcription](https://el.wikisource.org/w/index.php?title=Σελίδα%3AΑλεξανδρινή_Τέχνη_Τομ.1_Αρ.2_-_02.jpg&oldid=149663), including its final period and mixed Greek mu/micro-sign glyphs; editions are not spliced.
  - *Το πρώτο σκαλί* is a 26-line continuous reading on [p. 3](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Αλεξανδρινή_Τέχνη_Τομ.1_Αρ.2_-_03.jpg). The scan reads `εἶν’`, `νᾶσαι` and `ἀναιβῶ`; the [pinned page transcription](https://el.wikisource.org/w/index.php?title=Σελίδα%3AΑλεξανδρινή_Τέχνη_Τομ.1_Αρ.2_-_03.jpg&oldid=130131) has `εἲν’` and `νἆσαι`, while the linked schoolbook identifies a 1995 edition. The scan's closing punctuation is retained; indentation, spacing and some punctuation are normalized. Readings from different editions are not silently spliced.
  - [The issue's first page](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Αλεξανδρινή_Τέχνη_Τομ.1_Αρ.2_-_01.jpg) visibly says January 1927, although Commons image metadata says December 1927. The year identifies the printing, not necessarily composition. Egypt is an associated place, not a claim of composition there. The historical printing is public domain in the United States; copyright and moral rights vary by jurisdiction. Transcriptions are adapted from Ntetos and Greek Wikisource contributors under [CC BY-SA 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/), with linked revisions. Scans, translations and recordings are not republished. `python3 scripts/import-recognized-greek-classics-2.py --check` reproduces the [snapshot](../scripts/sources/recognized-greek-classics-2-readings.txt); optional `--evidence-dir <directory>` checks the three 1927 page images and the 1908 comparison PDF named in the importer. Hashes identify consulted copies, not transcription accuracy; duplicate screens cannot rule out every translation or paraphrase.

- Five individually documented Hungarian poems add **Sándor Petőfi**'s *Egy gondolat bánt engemet*, *Föltámadott a tenger*, *Az alföld* and *A Tisza*, and **Ferenc Kölcsey**'s *Hymnus*. Each complete poem is counted once, not once per edition, quotation, translation, anthem excerpt or musical setting. Hungarian examination-preparation materials discuss [*Egy gondolat bánt engemet*](https://erettsegi.com/tetelek/irodalom/petofi-sandor-forradalmi-versei-egy-gondolat-bant-engemet-elemzes/), [*Az alföld*](https://erettsegi.com/tetelek/irodalom/petofi-sandor-tajversei-az-alfold-elemzes/) and [*A Tisza*](https://erettsegi.com/tetelek/irodalom/petofi-sandor-a-tisza/) individually, and a [Petőfi survey](https://erettsegi.com/tetelek/irodalom/petofi-sandor-kolteszete/) names *Föltámadott a tenger*. The National Library of Hungary's [exhibition](https://oszk.hu/en/exhibitions/land-poems-hymn-appeal-national-song-our-emblematic-poems) identifies *Hymnus* among three emblematic poems. These document attention to individual works, not global rankings or permission to copy modern commentary, translations, photographs or recordings.
  - The four Petőfi readings follow [*Dalaim*, Gyoma, 1921](https://archive.org/download/dalaimantholgi00petf/dalaimantholgi00petf.pdf). *Egy gondolat bánt engemet* spans [pp. 17–18 / PDF pp. 21–22](https://archive.org/download/dalaimantholgi00petf/dalaimantholgi00petf.pdf#page=21), with a page turn after six of its 36 lines. *Föltámadott a tenger* spans [pp. 27–28 / PDF pp. 31–32](https://archive.org/download/dalaimantholgi00petf/dalaimantholgi00petf.pdf#page=31): its fourth five-line stanza crosses the page turn, and `Sűlyednek`, `fölűl` and `alúl` are retained from this printing. *Az alföld* has twelve quatrains on [pp. 123–125 / PDF pp. 127–129](https://archive.org/download/dalaimantholgi00petf/dalaimantholgi00petf.pdf#page=127), including `hosszu gémü`, `szinével` and `vadlúdak`. *A Tisza* has fifteen quatrains on [pp. 129–131 / PDF pp. 133–135](https://archive.org/download/dalaimantholgi00petf/dalaimantholgi00petf.pdf#page=133), with `fodrába` printed without an apostrophe. Historical spellings remain; heading ellipses, spacing and indentation are normalized.
  - *Hymnus* follows the [*Aurora* volume dated 1829, pp. 267–270 / PDF leaves 1–3](https://epa.oszk.hu/02900/02968/00002/pdf/EPA02968_aurora_1829_266-274.pdf#page=1). The [National Library chronology](https://erkel.oszk.hu/tan/hymnustol-himnuszig-avagy-egy-legitimacio-toertenete-%E2%80%93-evszamokban) reports that the volume was actually issued in December 1828. The eight-stanza first printing is signed KÖLCSEY and has no subtitle; the fifth stanza crosses pp. 268–269. It reads `Tokaj'`, `fenn fiad`, `Árvánk'`, `múltat` in its first stanza and `multat` in its last. The reading is not spliced with later editions; apostrophe typography and spacing are normalized. The linked OSZK scan and [modern digital presentation](https://dhupla.hu/text/Himnusz_nyomtatvany_oszk) are not republished.
  - *Dalaim* is volume IX of Kner-Klasszikusok and has a [signed 1921 afterword](https://archive.org/download/dalaimantholgi00petf/dalaimantholgi00petf.pdf#page=177) by **György Király**; the [Hungarian Biographical Lexicon](https://mek.oszk.hu/00300/00355/html/ABC07165/07907.htm) identifies him as the series editor. [BnF](https://catalogue.bnf.fr/ark:/12148/cb109620891) dates him 1887–1922. The afterword, decorative artwork and anthology arrangement are not copied.
  - The historical 1829-dated and 1921 printings are public domain in the United States; Kölcsey died in 1838, Petőfi in 1849 and Király in 1922. Copyright, edition-related and moral rights vary by jurisdiction; check local law. Neither a scan, translation nor recording is included. Dates identify printings rather than necessarily composition, and Hungary is an associated place rather than a claim about each poem's creation. No denomination or devotional classification is inferred from author identity. `python3 scripts/import-recognized-hungarian-poems.py --check` reproduces the [reading snapshot](../scripts/sources/recognized-hungarian-poems-readings.txt); optional `--evidence-dir <directory>` checks both PDF hashes. Hashes identify consulted copies rather than proving transcription accuracy; duplicate screens cannot rule out every translation or paraphrase.

- Two individually documented poems by **France Prešeren**, *Kam?* and *Slovo od mladosti*, follow Luka Pintar's [*Dóktorja Francéta Prešérna poezije*, third impression, 1914](https://archive.org/details/dktorjafranctap00pintgoog/page/n4/mode/1up). Each is one complete poem; translations, alternate readings, quotations and musical settings are not additional works. [Osnovna šola Metlika](https://www.osmetlika.si/files/2021/02/FRANCE-PRE%C5%A0EREN-%C5%BEivljenje-in-delo.pdf) names *Kam?* among Prešeren's known poems; [Encyclopaedia Britannica](https://www.britannica.com/biography/France-Preseren) discusses *Slovo od mladosti* individually, and the Slovenian association Zveza Sožitje [selects it for an accessible-reading series](https://www.zveza-sozitje.si/modules/uploader/uploads/news/files_news/Pesmi_Franceta_Pre_erna_knjiga_1_e_verzija_1.pdf). These are evidence of attention to the individual poems, not global rankings or permission to copy modern commentary, adaptations, translations or recordings.
  - *Kam?* is complete in six couplets on [p. 7 / PDF p. 18](https://archive.org/details/dktorjafranctap00pintgoog/page/n17/mode/1up), between *Prošnja* and *Ukazi*. The printed heading is `Kám?`; the text retains `Prijatli`, `raj’` / `raji` and `sèmtertjè`. The title is normalized for discovery; another spelling or translation is not a separate poem.
  - *Slovo od mladosti* has five eight-line stanzas on [pp. 87–89 / PDF pp. 98–100](https://archive.org/details/dktorjafranctap00pintgoog/page/n97/mode/1up), before *Glosa*. Its second and fifth stanzas cross page turns. The 1914 reading retains `solnce`, `rož’ce`, `d’janje`, `zanič’vati`, `golj’fijami` and `’Zučé`; it is not silently combined with a different edition.
  - The 1914 printing is public domain in the United States; Prešeren died in 1849 and Pintar in 1915. Copyright, edition-related and moral rights vary by jurisdiction; check local law. The scan is linked, not republished. Title accents, apostrophe typography, spacing and indentation are normalized; the text's other historical accents are retained. - Three individually documented Hungarian works add **Sándor Petőfi**'s *Szeptember végén* and *Nemzeti dal* and **Mihály Vörösmarty**'s *Szózat*. Each is one complete poem, not another work for a variant, translation, quotation, anthem excerpt or musical setting. The National Library of Hungary's [conference catalogue](https://mek.oszk.hu/17900/17913/cedula.html) documents individual attention to *Szeptember végén*; its [exhibition](https://oszk.hu/en/exhibitions/land-poems-hymn-appeal-national-song-our-emblematic-poems) names *Szózat* and *Nemzeti dal* among three emblematic poems. These are evidence of regional reception, not global rankings or permission to copy modern commentary, translations or recordings.
  - *Szeptember végén* follows [*Dalaim*, Gyoma, 1921, pp. 108–109 / PDF pp. 112–113](https://archive.org/download/dalaimantholgi00petf/dalaimantholgi00petf.pdf#page=112). Its three eight-line stanzas are complete; the second crosses the page turn and *Rózsabokor* follows. The printing retains `ifju`, `vegyűl`, `űlj`, `sirom`, `birhat`, `könyűimet` and `hivedet`.
  - *Nemzeti dal* follows the same anthology, [pp. 25–27 / PDF pp. 29–31](https://archive.org/download/dalaimantholgi00petf/dalaimantholgi00petf.pdf#page=29), before *Föltámadott a tenger*. The complete six eight-line stanzas include every refrain; the second crosses a page. The second oath of the fifth refrain prints `Eszküszünk`, apparently a typesetting error, and the fifth stanza also prints `hiréhez`; these readings are retained, not silently corrected using the distinct [1848 broadside](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Nemzeti_dal.jpg). The [Petőfi Literary Museum](https://pim.hu/muzeum/mutargyak/nemzeti-dal-ritka-kolozsvari-kiadasa-1848-bol) identifies it as perhaps the poet's best-known poem. The [National Museum](https://mnm.hu/muzeum/hirek/marcius-15-magyar-nemzeti-muzeumban-0) examines the legend that he recited it on its steps; that claim is not presented as established fact.
  - *Szózat* follows [*A merengőhöz*, Gyoma, 1921, pp. 67–69 / PDF pp. 71–73](https://archive.org/download/merenghz00vr/merenghz00vr.pdf#page=71). It contains fourteen complete quatrains before *Berzsenyi Emléke*; the seventh crosses a page turn and the last begins p. 69. The printing retains `rendületlenűl`, `kivűl`, `mellyen`, `hosszu`, `sűlyed`, `körűl` and `gyászköny`. A [1906 version](https://mek.oszk.hu/kiallitas/lenard/irasok/seta/sxg.html) differs and is not spliced into this reading.
  - The 1921 printings are public domain in the United States; Petőfi died in 1849 and Vörösmarty in 1855. Copyright, edition-related and moral rights vary by jurisdiction; check local law. Scans are linked, not republished. Printing dates do not necessarily establish composition dates; Hungary is an associated place, not a composition-location claim. A hash identifies a copy, not transcription accuracy, and duplicate screens cannot exclude every translation or paraphrase.

- Four individually documented British works use historical printings, each complete and counted once. The [secondary transcription of a 1995 BBC British reader poll](https://wykontario.org/index.php/features-category/1150-100-favorite-poems-of-britain) lists each individually; it is regional reception evidence, not a global ranking or a source of permission to copy later commentary, editions, recordings or translations.
  - W. H. Davies's *Leisure* appears as seven couplets in [*Songs of Joy and Others*, London, 1911, p. 15 / PDF p. 19](https://archive.org/download/songsofjoyothers00davi/songsofjoyothers00davi.pdf#page=19), before *Love's Power*. [Literature Wales and Swansea University](https://www.swansea.ac.uk/media/Leisure---W-H-Davies.pdf) describe the individual poem's extensive anthologizing. The commentary is not the source of the historical reading.
  - Rudyard Kipling's *The Way through the Woods* is separately headed and complete in [*Rewards and Fairies*, Toronto, 1910, p. 87 / PDF p. 107](https://archive.org/download/rewardsfairie00kipl/rewardsfairie00kipl.pdf#page=107), before *Marklake Witches*. The scan's title page says Toronto although the Archive metadata says London. The [Kipling Society](https://www.kiplingsociety.co.uk/readers-guide/rg_woods1.htm) calls it among his best-known poems. The 1910 text prints a period after `mate` and no comma after `woods` inside the parenthesis, unlike [a later online version](https://www.kiplingsociety.co.uk/poem/poems_woods.htm); readings are not spliced.
  - Kipling's *The Glory of the Garden* ends the [1911 Garden City, New York *A History of England*, pp. 306–308 / PDF pp. 390–392](https://archive.org/download/historyofengland00flet/historyofengland00flet.pdf#page=390). It is one separately headed poem with eight stanzas and a final repeated line, italic in the print and plain here. The [Kipling Society's notes](https://www.kiplingsociety.co.uk/readers-guide/rg_garden1.htm) discuss its school-history setting and later editions. Printer-wrapped lines are rejoined, not counted as additional lines or works.
  - G. K. Chesterton's *The Donkey* is four complete quatrains in [*The Wild Knight*, fourth edition, London and New York, 1914, pp. 16–17 / PDF pp. 34–35](https://archive.org/download/wildknight00chesrich/wildknight00chesrich.pdf#page=34); *The Beatific Vision* follows. The fourth edition is not asserted to be unchanged from the 1900 volume. The poem and its final allusive lines are one work, not a separate setting or devotional classification inferred from the author.
  - Printing years are not necessarily composition dates; associated places are not composition claims. Indentation and apostrophe typography are normalized, as are obvious printer wraps. - Two further complete **Rosalía de Castro** works follow the [University of Toronto copy of *Follas novas*, 1880](https://archive.org/details/follasnovasverso00castuoft). Each is one work, not an additional variant, section, translation or musical setting. Individual attention is documented below, not asserted as a global ranking.
  - *A xusticia pó-l-a man* occupies [pp. 57–58 / PDF pp. 93–94](https://archive.org/download/follasnovasverso00castuoft/follasnovasverso00castuoft.pdf#page=93), before another poem. Its 28 verse lines include the 1880 `Fiẍeronm’un leito` and `Y-a lua escondiese`; later versions often print `fixéronme un leito` and `I a lúa escondíase`. A printed rule is rendered as a separate dash and counted as a nonblank line, not verse. [María do Cebreiro Rábade Villar's essay](https://sibila.com.br/mapa-da-lingua/a-xustiza-pola-man-de-rosalia-de-castro/9718) analyzes the poem and identifies *La justicia por la mano* as Castro's Spanish self-translation, not a second work. [Universidade de Santiago](https://investigacion.usc.gal/documentos/5f0a474f299952723f772237) indexes a separate scholarly article; [Xunta de Galicia](https://www.lingua.gal/o-galego/promovelo/equipos-de-dinamizacion-da-lingua-galega/datas-sinaladas/listado/_/contido_0888/recitando-xustiza-pola-man-rosalia-castro) records a school recitation, and [AMariñaXa](https://amarinaxa.com/news/tres-institutos-da-marina-contaran-cun-mural-de-ana-santiso-inspirado-en-rosalia-de-castro/34933/) reports an initiative for poem-inspired murals in three Galician schools.
  - *¡Prá á Habana!* occupies [pp. 211–214 / PDF pp. 247–250](https://archive.org/download/follasnovasverso00castuoft/follasnovasverso00castuoft.pdf#page=247), before *¡Olvidemo-l-os mortos!*. The whole 80-verse poem has five numbered parts. The much-quoted `Este vaise y aquel vaise` starts part V, not a separate work; the five section labels are included and count as nonblank lines, not verse. The [Fundación Rosalía de Castro](https://rosalia.gal/rosalia-na-coruna/) calls this individual poem emblematic and discusses its treatment of emigration.
  - The 1880 printing is public domain in the United States; Castro died in 1885. Copyright, edition-related and moral rights vary by jurisdiction; check local law. The scan is linked, not republished; no translation or recording is included. Historical spelling, accents and `ẍ` are retained; indentation, italics, paragraph spacing and apostrophe typography are normalized. Modern and translated-title aliases are search metadata, not additional texts or works. The printing year is not necessarily the composition date. - Three individually documented Galician works by **Rosalía de Castro** follow the [1863 *Cantares gallegos*, Boston Public Library copy](https://archive.org/download/cantaresgallegos00cast_0/cantaresgallegos00cast_0.pdf). Its title page dates the Vigo printing. The [Fundación Rosalía de Castro and Universidade de Vigo](https://rosalia.gal/planeta-rosalia/) name *Adiós ríos, adiós fontes* and *Airiños, airiños aires* among the collection's best-known poems; [Turismo de Galicia](https://blog.turismo.gal/as-campas-de-bastavales-as-que-cantou-rosalia/) describes the opening of *Campanas de Bastabales* as well known and recited. These are evidence of individual reception, not global rankings or permission to copy modern commentary, translations or recordings.
  - *Adiós* is no. 13, [pp. 57–59 / PDF pp. 81–83](https://archive.org/download/cantaresgallegos00cast_0/cantaresgallegos00cast_0.pdf#page=81). This complete 1863 printing ends `¡Miña casiña! meu lar!`; [later editions](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/59037) contain additional and altered stanzas, which are not silently spliced into it. A row of dots in the 1863 printing is represented as spaced dots and counted as a nonblank line, not a verse.
  - *Airiños* is no. 15, [pp. 65–68 / PDF pp. 89–92](https://archive.org/download/cantaresgallegos00cast_0/cantaresgallegos00cast_0.pdf#page=89); no. 16 follows. The 1863 text preserves `Mozos`, `murmuxando` and `Éivos`; the foundation's [online selection](https://rosalia.gal/planeta-rosalia/airinos-ao-espanol/) is excerpted and differs in spelling. Its separately credited Spanish translation is not reproduced.
  - *Campanas* is no. 9, [pp. 39–43 / PDF pp. 63–67](https://archive.org/download/cantaresgallegos00cast_0/cantaresgallegos00cast_0.pdf#page=63). The five numbered sections form one work; the three-line opening returns at the end. Section numbers and a printed dotted separator are represented in the text and count as nonblank lines, not verse. `Bastavales` and `oio` are modern search aliases, not different works. The [Real Academia Galega](https://academia.gal/-/campanas-de-bastavales-a-saudade-feita-corpo-que-camina) discusses this individual poem. The [Ciudad Seva version](https://ciudadseva.com/texto/campanas-de-bastabales/) is a Spanish translation without an identified translator; it is not used as the source of this reading or reproduced.
  - Each printed poem begins with a separately italicized copla, included as an introductory block, not counted as a separate poem. The relationship of these individual openings to traditional oral sources is not independently established here; their authorship is not separately asserted. Italics, indentation, apostrophe typography and dot spacing are normalized; the historical wording is not modernized. Dates identify the printing, not necessarily composition. Spain is an associated place, not a claim about the composition location.

- Four individually documented works add **Luo Yin**'s *蜂*, **Rosalía de Castro**'s *Dicen que no hablan las plantas*, **Gerard Manley Hopkins**'s *God’s Grandeur* and **William Butler Yeats**'s *The Song of Wandering Aengus*. Each is a complete work counted once, not again for variants, translations, quotations or settings. The educational and literary selections and the secondary poll transcription document individual attention, not global rankings or permission to copy modern commentary. No translations, recordings or inferred religious classifications are added.
  - Luo's heading is on [*御定全唐詩*, 四庫全書, 卷六百六十二, PDF p. 20](https://archive.org/download/06060099.cn/06060099.cn.pdf#page=20); the quatrain appears on p. 21 before *簾二首*. Page 2 identifies Luo. A small note gives `不知` as an alternate to `為誰`, not an additional line. The [Classical Chinese Literature selection](https://www.classicalchineseliterature.org/article.php?article=%E8%9C%82) discusses the poem individually and credits Hong Kong Education Bureau materials. Line breaks and punctuation are supplied and historical glyphs normalized; simplified and alternate search aliases are not translations. Archive metadata describes a reproduction of the Qing collection but does not identify the particular manuscript or reproduction date; the scanned pages are linked, not republished.
  - Castro's otherwise untitled *Dicen que no hablan las plantas* occupies an ornamented page of [*En las orillas del Sar*, Madrid (1884), p. 109 / PDF p. 111](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/8/82/En_las_orillas_del_Sar_1884.pdf#page=111). The preceding page is blank; the following page begins `Cada vez que recuerda tanto oprobio` under an asterisk. [Ciudad Seva](https://ciudadseva.com/texto/dicen-que-no-hablan-las-plantas/) independently presents the first text as a complete individual poem; the [Fundación Rosalía de Castro](https://rosalia.gal/rosalia/citas-e-frases/dicen-que-no-hablan-las-plantas/) selects an excerpt, not another work. The 1884 reading retains `murmureis`, the unaccented second `como` in the last question, and the short `Ahí va la loca` line; spacing is normalized. A [1909 Wikisource page](https://es.wikisource.org/wiki/Dicen_que_no_hablan_las_plantas,_ni_las_fuentes,_ni_los_p%C3%A1jaros) combines several asterisk-separated texts under the first opening; it is not the source for this reading or a reason to count its other texts.
  - Hopkins's complete fourteen-line [*God’s Grandeur*, *Poems of Gerard Manley Hopkins*, p. 26 / PDF p. 40](https://archive.org/download/poemsofgerardman030114mbp/poemsofgerardman030114mbp.pdf#page=40), precedes *The Starlight Night*. The title page is undated; the [National Library of Ireland](https://catalogue.nli.ie/Record/vtls000163758) catalogues Robert Bridges's edition as 1918. Printer-wrapped lines are rejoined and spacing and indentation normalized; the date is the edition's, not necessarily composition.
  - Yeats's [*The Song of Wandering Aengus*, *The Wind Among the Reeds* (1899), pp. 15–16 / PDF pp. 27–28](https://archive.org/download/windamongreeds00yeat/windamongreeds00yeat.pdf#page=27), has three eight-line stanzas, with the second crossing a page turn; *The Song of the Old Mother* follows on p. 17. The author's separately printed prose note is not another stanza or work. The [Academy of American Poets](https://poets.org/poem/song-wandering-aengus) selects the poem individually. A [secondary transcription](https://wykontario.org/index.php/features-category/1150-100-favorite-poems-of-britain) of a 1995 BBC British poll lists this work and *God’s Grandeur*, evidence of regional reception rather than a worldwide ranking.
  - These historical texts and selected readings are public domain in the United States; copyright, edition-related and moral rights vary elsewhere, so check local law. Scans are linked, not republished. Associated countries do not assert composition locations. `python3 scripts/import-recognized-world-classics-8.py --check` reproduces the [reading snapshot](../scripts/sources/recognized-world-classics-8-readings.txt); optional `--evidence-dir <directory>` verifies four source-copy hashes. Hashes identify copies, not transcription accuracy; duplicate screens cannot exclude every translation or paraphrase.

- Seven individually documented works add **Li Qingzhao**'s *烏江絕句*, **Lu You**'s *示兒*, **Ouyang Xiu**'s *畫眉鳥*, **Rainer Maria Rilke**'s *Herbst*, and **William Butler Yeats**'s *The Wild Swans at Coole*, *An Irish Airman Foresees His Death* and *The Stolen Child*. Each is a complete work counted once, not again for variants, translations, quotations or settings. The linked educational selections and literary discussions document attention to the individual poems, not global rankings or permission to reproduce modern commentary or translations. No translations, recordings, inferred religious classifications or composition-place claims are added.
  - Li's quatrain is quoted in her entry in [*詩女史*, 明嘉靖, 卷十一, PDF p. 24](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:NLC892-2408-209620_詩女史_第5冊.pdf?page=24) as `烏江絕句云`, followed after its four lines by `分得知字云`; it is not separately laid out as a headed poem on that page. [Skyed Press](https://www.skyedpress.com.hk/lite_new/asp_t2/03_essay/download/essay/html/1266.htm) discusses *烏江* individually and gives *夏日絕句* as another title. The original title and exact composition date are not established here; the 16th-century compilation is not an authorial manuscript.
  - Lu's separately headed *示兒* ends 卷八十五 of [*劍南詩稿*, late-Ming printing with later notes, PDF p. 128](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:NLC892-411999023439-306879_劍南詩稿_第24冊.pdf?page=128), immediately before the postscript. The historical witness reads `死去元知` and `家祭無忘`; the [Hong Kong Education Bureau](https://www.edb.gov.hk/attachment/tc/curriculum-development/kla/chi-edu/resources/primary/lang/culture/03_042.pdf) teaches the same poem with `死去原知` and `家祭毋忘`. Another Lu poem shares the title *示兒*; the opening distinguishes this work.
  - Ouyang's *畫眉鳥* follows [*歐陽文忠公集*, 明正統, 卷十一, PDF pp. 1 and 8](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:NLC892-1613-208408_歐陽文忠公集_第2冊.pdf?page=8), ending before *懷嵩樓新開南軒與郡僚小飲*. Notes give `郡齋聞百舌` as an alternate heading and `任` for `隨`; these are not extra verse or separate works. The [Education Bureau](https://www.edb.gov.hk/attachment/tc/curriculum-development/kla/chi-edu/resources/primary/lang/jilei_shi_pdf/jilei_shi_071.pdf) discusses the poem individually. For the Chinese readings, line breaks and punctuation are supplied and historical glyph forms normalized; simplified and variant search aliases are not translations.
  - Rilke's complete nine-line [*Herbst*, *Das Buch der Bilder*, second edition (1906), p. 51](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Das_Buch_der_Bilder_(Rilke)_051.jpg), is a different poem from the already catalogued *Herbsttag*. The source reads `Einer welcher` without a comma, unlike some later printings. [Literary Matters](https://www.literarymatters.org/12-1-rainer-maria-rilke-fall/) selects this individual poem for a modern translation, which is not included; a [Deutschlandfunk poetry-calendar discussion](https://www.planetlyrik.de/lyrikkalender/rainer-maria-rilkes-gedicht-herbst) also discusses it individually, although an automated fetch was blocked.
  - Yeats's five-stanza [*The Wild Swans at Coole*, Cuala Press (1917), pp. 1–2 / PDF pp. 17–18](https://archive.org/download/wildswansatcoole00yeatrich/wildswansatcoole00yeatrich.pdf#page=17), precedes *Men Improve with the Years*. The witness has `nineteenth Autumn` and `Delight men's eyes when I awake some day`. [Yale's Modernism Lab](https://campuspress.yale.edu/modernismlab/the-wild-swans-at-coole/) discusses this individual poem.
  - [*An Irish Airman Foresees His Death*, New York Macmillan (1919), p. 13 / PDF p. 29](https://archive.org/download/wild00swansatcooleyeatrich/wild00swansatcooleyeatrich.pdf#page=29), has sixteen complete lines. The 1919 witness reads `Nor public man, nor angry crowds`; other readings use `men` and/or `cheering`. The [Poetry Society](https://resources.poetrysociety.org.uk/lesson/yeats-an-irish-airman-foresees-his-death/) teaches the individual poem; its modern text is not spliced into this witness.
  - [*The Stolen Child*, *The Poetical Works*, New York Macmillan, pp. 39–42 / PDF pp. 59–62](https://archive.org/download/cu31924013575083/cu31924013575083.pdf#page=59), has four complete stanzas before *To an Isle in the Water*. The inspected title page is dated **1920**, although its copyright page says 1906 and identifies November 1906 as publication; this copy is not assumed to reproduce the 1906 text unchanged. Its punctuation includes `vats.` and `streams,`, it reads `And is anxious in its sleep`, and its last refrain begins `From a world`; [the Academy of American Poets](https://poets.org/poem/stolen-child) selects a different reading. The source's italic refrains are plain text here; printer-wrapped lines are rejoined. A supposedly 1889 Wikisource transcription does not identify its source and was not used as an 1889 witness.
  - These historical printings and selected readings are public domain in the United States; copyright, edition-related and moral rights vary elsewhere, so check local law. The National Library of China scans are marked public domain on Commons; scans are linked, not republished. Spacing and indentation are normalized, and dates identify printings rather than necessarily composition dates. `python3 scripts/import-recognized-world-classics-7.py --check` reproduces the [reading snapshot](../scripts/sources/recognized-world-classics-7-readings.txt); optional `--evidence-dir <directory>` verifies seven source-copy hashes. Hashes identify copies, not transcription accuracy; duplicate screens cannot exclude every translation or paraphrase.

- Three individually documented poems by **Dan Andersson** add *Omkring tiggarn från Luossa*, *En spelmans jordafärd* and *Till min syster*. Each complete poem is counted once, not again for quotations, translations, variants or musical settings. Sweden is an associated place, not a claim about composition locations; no translations, recordings or inferred religious classifications are added.
  - The readings follow the [1921 *Visor och ballader*](https://runeberg.org/danvob/), which includes *Svarta ballader*. The scanned [title page](https://runeberg.org/danvob/0005.html) dates the printing; its year is not necessarily the composition date. Printer-wrapped verse lines are rejoined, and spacing, indentation and dash typography normalized. The historical text is transcribed from the page images, not from a modern edited text or recording.
  - *Omkring tiggarn från Luossa* has nine quatrains on [pp. 81–83](https://runeberg.org/danvob/0083.html). The scanned [contents](https://runeberg.org/danvob/0248.html) names it at p. 81, although that page does not itself have a heading; *En spelmans jordafärd* starts on p. 84. The well-known `Det är något bortom bergen` passage is part of the same poem, not an additional work.
  - *En spelmans jordafärd* has five eight-line stanzas and a final nine-line stanza on [pp. 84–86](https://runeberg.org/danvob/0086.html); *Spelmannen* starts on p. 87. The 1921 reading has `Himmelmora kamm`, `säj`, `han led ofta brist`, `så stora tunga steg` and `solens bleka ro`. A [separate electronic text](https://runeberg.org/svartbal/02.html) reads `kam`, `sej`, `ofta av brist`, `så tunga steg` and `skogens bleka ro`. Runeberg [identifies a 1989 collected edition](https://runeberg.org/authors/danander.html) as its source for these early electronic texts, so it is not treated as the 1921 printing. `kam` is a search alias, not a separate work.
  - *Till min syster* has four quatrains on [pp. 117–118](https://runeberg.org/danvob/0119.html), before *En gamling*. The 1921 printing retains `däj` and `det starka oss`; `dej` is a search alias. Its printed line breaks across page width are rejoined without adding words.
  - [Litteraturbanken's translator lexicon](https://litteraturbanken.se/oversattarlexikon/artiklar/Dan_Andersson) identifies all three as among Andersson's best-known poems. Its school pages separately teach [the beggar poem](https://litteraturbanken.se/skolan/poesi-forfattare-dan-andersson-omkring-tiggarn-fran-luossa/), [the funeral](https://litteraturbanken.se/skolan/poesi-forfattare-dan-andersson-en-spelmans-jordafard/) and [*Till min syster*](https://litteraturbanken.se/skolan/poesi-forfattare-dan-andersson-till-min-syster/). These document Swedish reception, not a global ranking or permission to copy modern commentary or recordings.
  - The 1921 printing is public domain in the United States; Andersson died in 1920. Copyright, edition-related and moral rights vary elsewhere; check local law. The facsimile is linked, not republished. `python3 scripts/import-recognized-world-classics-6.py --check` reproduces the [reading snapshot](../scripts/sources/recognized-world-classics-6-readings.txt); optional `--evidence-dir <directory>` verifies ten scanned-page hashes. Hashes identify the consulted copies, not transcription accuracy; duplicate screens cannot exclude every translation or paraphrase.

- Five individually documented works add **Su Shi**'s *題西林壁*, **Cao Ye**'s *官倉鼠*, **Yu Shinan**'s *詠螢*, and **Dan Andersson**'s *Jag väntar…* and *Hälgdagskväll i timmerkojan*. Each is a complete, separately headed poem counted once, not again for variants, translations, quotations or musical settings. The Hong Kong Education Bureau's selections and Litteraturbanken's individual teaching and reading selections document regional attention, not global rankings or permission to copy modern commentary or recordings. No translations, recordings, inferred religious classifications or composition-place claims are added.
  - Su's *題西林壁* follows [*蘇詩補註*, 四庫全書, 卷二十三, PDF p. 14](https://archive.org/download/06056059.cn/06056059.cn.pdf#page=14), before *西林*. The Qing-era collection names editor 查慎行. Its main reading is `遠近高低各不同`; small notes give `看山了` and `無一` as alternatives, not extra verse. The poem also occurs in [*記遊廬山*](https://zh.wikisource.org/wiki/記遊盧山); the prose quotation is not a second poem. The [Hong Kong Education Bureau](https://www.edb.gov.hk/attachment/tc/curriculum-development/kla/chi-edu/resources/primary/lang/jilei_shi_pdf/jilei_shi_079.pdf) discusses the work individually.
  - Cao's *官倉鼠* follows [*御定全唐詩*, 四庫全書, 卷五百九十二, PDF pp. 107 and 119–120](https://archive.org/download/06060085.cn/06060085.cn.pdf#page=120). Page 107 identifies the author; the quatrain follows *望不來* and precedes *題濮廟*. The main text reads `大如斗`, with a small `一作牛` alternative that is not verse. The [Hong Kong Education Bureau](https://www.edb.gov.hk/attachment/tc/curriculum-development/kla/chi-edu/resources/primary/lang/jilei_shi_pdf/jilei_shi_069.pdf) discusses this poem individually.
  - Yu's *詠螢* follows [*御定全唐詩*, 卷三十六, PDF pp. 14–15](https://archive.org/download/06063882.cn/06063882.cn.pdf#page=14). Its heading ends p. 14, and the complete quatrain precedes *蟬* on p. 15. The historical witness reads `的歷` and `飄颻`; the [Hong Kong selection](https://www.edb.gov.hk/attachment/tc/curriculum-development/kla/chi-edu/resources/primary/lang/jilei_shi_pdf/jilei_shi_012.pdf) also explains `飄搖` as an equivalent. For all three Chinese poems, line breaks and punctuation are supplied and some historical glyphs normalized. Simplified-script and variant search aliases are not separate editions or translations.
  - Andersson's [*Jag väntar…*, *Kolvaktarens visor* (1915), pp. 33–34](https://litteraturbanken.se/forfattare/AnderssonD/titlar/KolvaktarensVisor/sida/33/faksimil), contains four complete quatrains before *Till kvinnan* on p. 35. The printing retains the comma after `blomma`; printer-wrapped lines are rejoined. [Litteraturbanken's author page](https://litteraturbanken.se/ljudochbild/forfattare/anderssond/) individually selects the poem for a spoken reading, whose recording is not reproduced. A [Swedish literary review](https://www.dagensarena.se/innehall/pa-vandring-dan-anderssons-okanda-bohemliv) calls it one of Andersson's best-known poems, and a [Ludvika public-art selection](https://dananderssonveckan.com/citaten) independently names a line from it; neither is a global ranking or the source of this transcription.
  - Andersson's [*Hälgdagskväll i timmerkojan*, pp. 73–75](https://litteraturbanken.se/forfattare/AnderssonD/titlar/KolvaktarensVisor/sida/73/faksimil), contains nine complete quatrains before *När Gasken dog*. The 1915 title and first stanza read `Hälgdagskväll` and `hälg`, while [Litteraturbanken's school selection](https://litteraturbanken.se/skolan/poesi-forfattare-dan-andersson-heldagskvall-i-timmerkojan/) reads `Helgdagskväll` and `helg`. The historical reading has `spela en vals`; a [separate online transcription](https://runeberg.org/kolvakt/25.html) omits `en`. The two are not spliced or counted as separate works. Dash typography, spacing and indentation are normalized for both Swedish poems.
  - These historical texts and selected readings are public domain in the United States; Andersson died in 1920. Copyright, edition-related and moral rights vary by jurisdiction; check local law. Litteraturbanken's [facsimile metadata](https://litteraturbanken.se/api/get_work_info?authorid=AnderssonD&titlepath=KolvaktarensVisor&exclude=content_vector) marks its copy CC0; scans are linked, not republished. The deterministic importer and reading snapshot (`scripts/import-recognized-world-classics-5.py` and `scripts/sources/recognized-world-classics-5-readings.txt`) identify inspected copies by SHA-256; a hash does not establish transcription accuracy.

- Six individually documented works add **Percy Bysshe Shelley**'s *Ode to the West Wind*, **Gerard Manley Hopkins**'s *The Windhover* and *Pied Beauty*, **Georg Trakl**'s *Grodek*, and **Fyodor Tyutchev**'s *Silentium* and *Весенняя гроза*. Each is counted once, not again for variants, translations, quotations or settings. An English anthology survey, individual guides and literary studies document attention to these works; they are not global rankings or licenses to copy modern commentary. No translations, recordings or inferred religious classifications are added.
  - Shelley's complete [*Ode to the West Wind*, *Prometheus Unbound* (1820), pp. 188–192 / PDF pp. 192–196](https://archive.org/download/prometheusunboun00shelrich/prometheusunboun00shelrich.pdf#page=192), has five fourteen-line sections. The section numbers remain in the reader and count as nonblank lines; the 70 verse lines are one poem. The following page begins another ode. The source retains `O, wild`, `every where`, `airy`, `grey`, `uncontroulable` and `Oh! lift`. Shelley's printed two-paragraph prose footnote is attributed in the reader context, not counted as verse; its historical claim about underwater vegetation is not a scientific assertion by the library. The [Poetry Foundation's unscientific 17-anthology survey](https://www.poetryfoundation.org/articles/68445/hit-singles) lists the ode individually.
  - Hopkins's [*The Windhover*, p. 29 / PDF p. 43](https://archive.org/download/poemsofgerardman030114mbp/poemsofgerardman030114mbp.pdf#page=43), and [*Pied Beauty*, p. 30 / PDF p. 44](https://archive.org/download/poemsofgerardman030114mbp/poemsofgerardman030114mbp.pdf#page=44), are separately headed in Robert Bridges's first edited collection. The [National Library of Ireland](https://catalogue.nli.ie/Record/vtls000163758) catalogues it as 1918, although the scanned title page lacks a printed year. The former's dedication is `To Christ our Lord`; it retains `ecstacy`, `shéer plód`, `gold-vermillion` and the intended `king-` / `dom` verse break. The complete eleven-line curtal sonnet *Pied Beauty* retains `áll trádes` and `fathers-forth`. Printer wraps within lines are rejoined; small capitals, indentation and punctuation spacing are normalized. The [Poetry Foundation](https://www.poetryfoundation.org/articles/69191/gerard-manley-hopkins-the-windhover) and [EBSCO](https://www.ebsco.com/research-starters/history/pied-beauty-gerard-manley-hopkins) discuss the poems individually; their modern texts and commentary are not our transcription.
  - Trakl's complete [*Grodek*, *Brenner-Jahrbuch* (1915), p. 14](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Trakl_grodek.jpg), retains the posthumous Ludwig von Ficker printing's `tötlichen` and `Düster`, rather than the `tödlichen` and `Düstrer` found in later editions. Two long verse lines are rejoined across printer wraps, and the printed `(September 1914)` is a date label, not verse. [EBSCO](https://www.ebsco.com/research-starters/history/grodek-georg-trakl/) discusses the 17-line poem and its reception; an [individual scholarly study](https://www.persee.fr/doc/reger_0399-1989_1995_num_25_1_1183) uses a different edition. Ficker died in 1967, so edition-related rights outside the United States need separate consideration.
  - Tyutchev's [*Silentium*, p. 12 / PDF p. 190](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Sovremennik_1854_03-04.pdf?page=190), and [*Весенняя гроза*, p. 24 / PDF p. 202](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Sovremennik_1854_03-04.pdf?page=202), follow the separately dated and attributed 1854 *Стихотворения Ф. Тютчева* printed within *Современник*. Each is separately headed and complete between numbered neighboring poems. The 1854 *Silentium* has `И всходят и зайдут оне / Как звезды ясные в ночи` and `Дневные ослепят лучи`; an [1836 printing](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Sovremennik_1836_03.pdf?page=18) differs, but is not another work. The four-quatrain *Весенняя гроза* includes the final Hebe stanza; its `ветренная` is retained. Pre-reform Cyrillic letters, adjective endings and terminal hard signs are modernized, as is spacing; the printed `оне` is retained. The [Guardian's selection](https://www.theguardian.com/books/2015/feb/21/saturday-poem-silentium-by-fyodor-tyutchev-robert-chandler) and an [Oryol State University study](https://cyberleninka.ru/article/n/mifologicheskiy-podtekst-stihotvoreniya-f-i-tyutcheva-vesennyaya-groza) document individual reception, not permission to copy the modern translation or commentary.
  - Historical printings and selected readings are public domain in the United States, not a determination of worldwide status. Copyright, edition-related and moral rights vary by jurisdiction; check local law. Scans are linked, not republished. The deterministic importer and reading snapshot (`scripts/import-recognized-world-classics-4.py` and `scripts/sources/recognized-world-classics-4-readings.txt`) identify inspected copies by SHA-256; a hash does not establish transcription accuracy.

- Eight individually documented works add **Zhang Ruoxu**'s *春江花月夜*, **Bai Juyi**'s *暮江吟*, **Sikong Shu**'s *江村即事*, **Wang Jian**'s *小松*, **Rubén Darío**'s *A Roosevelt* and *Canción de otoño en primavera*, **Luis de Góngora**'s Soneto X, and **Eino Leino**'s *Rauhattoman rukous*. Each is a complete, separately headed poem counted once, not again for variants, translations, refrains, quotations or settings. The linked education selections, individual studies and literary-society discussion document attention to these particular works; they are not global rankings or licenses to copy modern commentary. No translations, recordings, inferred religious classifications or composition-place claims are added.
  - Zhang's 36-line *春江花月夜* follows [*御定全唐詩*, 四庫全書, 卷一百十七, PDF pp. 132–134](https://archive.org/download/06063895.cn/06063895.cn.pdf#page=132), which identifies the author and places *代答閨夢還* next. Its last line prints `落月遥情滿江樹`, with the historical glyph normalized to `遙`; the [Hong Kong Education Bureau selection](https://www.edb.gov.hk/attachment/tc/curriculum-development/kla/chi-edu/resources/primary/lang/jilei_shi_pdf/jilei_shi_017.pdf) reads `搖情`. The scan also prints small alternate-reading notes for `里`/`頃`, `妝`/`玉` and `玉`/`遮`; these are not extra verse. `沈沈` is retained, while other historical glyph forms are normalized. This title was an older shared song-title; a different poem bearing it is not automatically the same work.
  - *暮江吟* follows [卷四百四十二, PDF pp. 140–141](https://archive.org/download/06060050.cn/06060050.cn.pdf#page=140), ending before *思婦眉* and reading `可憐九月初三夜` and `真珠`. [EDB](https://www.edb.gov.hk/attachment/tc/curriculum-development/kla/chi-edu/resources/primary/lang/jilei_shi_pdf/jilei_shi_054.pdf) describes competing composition accounts. *江村即事* follows [卷二百九十二, PDF pp. 83–84](https://archive.org/download/06060020.cn/06060020.cn.pdf#page=83), ending that volume; *小松* follows [卷三百一, PDF p. 6](https://archive.org/download/06060022.cn/06060022.cn.pdf#page=6), before *秋夜*. [EDB discusses *江村即事*](https://www.edb.gov.hk/attachment/tc/curriculum-development/kla/chi-edu/resources/primary/lang/jilei_shi_pdf/jilei_shi_046.pdf) and [*小松*](https://www.edb.gov.hk/attachment/tc/curriculum-development/kla/chi-edu/resources/primary/lang/jilei_shi_pdf/jilei_shi_049.pdf) individually and notes the latter's `傍`/`旁` variant. Chinese line breaks and punctuation are supplied, and simplified-script search aliases are not separate editions.
  - Darío's [*A Roosevelt*, printed pp. 37–39 / PDF pp. 53–55](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Cantos_de_vida_y_esperanza_(1905).pdf?page=53), and [*Canción de otoño en primavera*, pp. 85–88 / PDF pp. 105–108](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Cantos_de_vida_y_esperanza_(1905).pdf?page=105), follow the 1905 *Cantos de vida y esperanza*. A Roosevelt's historical generalizations about peoples are the poet's rhetoric, not the library's claims; a [University of Seville study](https://doi.org/10.12795/ph.2020.v34.i02.06) discusses the poem and its racial and sociocultural rhetoric. The 1905 printing retains `á`, `ó`, unpaired exclamation marks, `Nueva-York`, and the unaccented spellings `Netzahualcoyotl` and `Eden`.
  - *Canción* has seventeen quatrains plus its final `Mas es mía el Alba de oro!`, with the printed dedication `A Martínez Sierra`; the following title is *Trébol*. The 1905 second-love stanza begins `La otra fué más sensitiva / Y más consoladora y más / Halagadora y expresiva`; a [1918 transcription](https://es.wikisource.org/wiki/Canci%C3%B3n_de_oto%C3%B1o_en_primavera) orders these lines differently. The repeated refrain, variants and final line are not separate works. A small mark after `ligera` is unclear in the 1905 scan and is not treated as punctuation. A [Université libre de Bruxelles bibliography](https://difusion.ulb.ac.be/vufind/Record/ULB-DIPOT:oai:dipot.ulb.ac.be:2013/105934/Details) identifies a study of this poem, not a license for a translation.
  - Góngora's complete Soneto X follows [*Todas las obras*, Madrid, 1654, PDF p. 38](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Todas_las_obras_de_Don_Lvis_de_Gongora_-_en_varios_poemas_(IA_todaslasobrasded00gong).pdf?page=38), collected by Gonzalo de Hozes y Cordoua, and was compared with the [Seville copy, PDF p. 44](https://archive.org/download/A002084/A002084.pdf#page=44). The 1654 witness has `el Sol`, `luciente marfil`, `marfil luciente` and `en tierra, en humo, en polvo`, unlike familiar modern readings. The opening is added to the catalogue title; long-s, u/v, ç/z, spacing and accents are normalized, and punctuation and sonnet stanza breaks are supplied independently. [Oxford's teaching selection](https://new.ox.ac.uk/sites/default/files/2025-08/Spanish%20New%20FPE%20GA%20Sonnets%202025%20compressed.pdf) identifies the sonnet individually; its modern transcription is not the source of this reading.
  - Leino's *Rauhattoman rukous* follows [*Hiihtäjän virsiä*, Helsinki, 1900, pp. 74–76 / PDF pp. 84–86](https://digi.kansalliskirjasto.fi/teos/binding/1907469/pdf/Hiiht%C3%A4j%C3%A4n_virsi%C3%A4_Eino_Leino_01_01_1900.pdf#page=84). All six eight-line stanzas are included; the fifth crosses a page and *Mua pelottaa* begins next. The [Kainuu Eino Leino society](https://www.kainuuneinoleinoseura.fi/eino-leino/runoteokset/) names it among the volume's well-known poems. The witness's `joulu-yö`, `tääll’` and `nuor’` are retained; apostrophe typography and indentation are normalized.
  - These historical printings and selected readings are public domain in the United States, not a determination of worldwide status. Copyright, edition-related and moral rights vary by jurisdiction; check local law. Scans are linked, not republished. The deterministic importer and reading snapshot (`scripts/import-recognized-world-classics-3.py` and `scripts/sources/recognized-world-classics-3-readings.txt`) identify inspected copies by SHA-256; a hash does not establish transcription accuracy.

- Seven individually documented works add **Wang Anshi**'s *泊船瓜洲*, **Su Shunqin**'s *淮中晚泊犢頭*, **Garcilaso de la Vega**'s Sonnets V and XIII, **Arthur Rimbaud**'s *Sensation*, **Joachim du Bellay**'s Sonnet XXXI and **Alphonse de Lamartine**'s *Le Lac de B\*\*\**. These are complete, separately headed poems, each counted once regardless of alternate readings, translations, quotations or settings. The Hong Kong Education Bureau's individual recitation selections, Oxford's individual sonnet teaching selection, a literary essay and French poetry selections document attention to the works; they are not global rankings or permission to copy modern commentary. No translations, recordings, inferred religious classifications or composition-place claims are added.
  - *泊船瓜洲* follows [*臨川文集*, 四庫全書 witness, 卷二十九, PDF p. 90](https://archive.org/download/06053553.cn/06053553.cn.pdf#page=90). The complete quatrain is separately headed and ends before *重過余婆岡市* on the next page. The witness reads `春風自綠江南岸`; the [Hong Kong recitation selection](https://www.edb.gov.hk/attachment/tc/curriculum-development/kla/chi-edu/resources/primary/lang/jilei_shi_pdf/jilei_shi_076.pdf) reads `春風又綠江南岸`. A quotation in an earlier poem and these textual variants are not separate works.
  - *淮中晚泊犢頭* follows [*皇朝文鑑*, 四部叢刊, 卷二十七, p. 43](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Sibu_Congkan1979-呂祖謙-皇朝文鑑-40-10.djvu?page=43), a reproduction of a Song printing. The next page starts *暑中閑詠*; the exact 四部叢刊 impression has not been independently dated. `隂` and `㸔` are normalized to `陰` and `看`. The [Hong Kong recitation selection](https://www.edb.gov.hk/attachment/tc/curriculum-development/kla/chi-edu/resources/primary/lang/jilei_shi_pdf/jilei_shi_072.pdf) discusses the poem individually. Chinese verse breaks and punctuation are supplied; simplified-script and variant search aliases are not separate editions.
  - Garcilaso's Sonnets [V, p. 211](https://archive.org/details/obra00vega/page/n238/mode/1up) and [XIII, p. 220](https://archive.org/details/obra00vega/page/n247/mode/1up) follow the 1911 *Obras*, with the opening lines added to their catalogue titles and editorial sonnet stanza breaks supplied. The first retains `escrebir` / `escrebistes` and the historical accent in `á`; the second has `que al oro` / `cresca`. [Oxford's individual teaching selection](https://new.ox.ac.uk/sites/default/files/2025-08/Spanish%20New%20FPE%20GA%20Sonnets%202025%20compressed.pdf) uses different readings, including `qu’el oro` / `crezca`; its modern text is not our transcription. The 1911 printing is public domain in the United States, but its editor Tomás Navarro Tomás died in 1979 and edition-related rights may differ elsewhere.
  - *Sensation* follows [Rimbaud's 1895 *Poésies complètes*, p. 53](https://archive.org/details/bub_gb_EZA7AQAAMAAJ/page/n73/mode/1up), with two complete quatrains before *Bal des pendus*. The printed `Mars 1870` under the poem is not independently established here as its composition date. This witness uses a semicolon after `rien` and an exclamation at the end. A [LittPo essay](https://littpo.fr/2020/12/16/sensation-de-rimbaud/) discusses the individual poem, not the license for our transcription.
  - Du Bellay's *Les Regrets* Sonnet XXXI follows the [Séché volume III, p. 44](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Du_Bellay_-_Œuvres_complètes,_édition_Séché,_tome_3.djvu?page=56), between separately numbered XXX and XXXII; sonnet stanza breaks and the catalogue's opening-line title are supplied. The [visible volume title page](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Du_Bellay_-_Œuvres_complètes,_édition_Séché,_tome_3.djvu?page=7) says **1910** although Commons metadata says **1903**. The printing retains `reverray`, `cheminee`, `Loyre` and the periods after `ayeux` and `fine`. An [Études littéraires guide](https://www.etudes-litteraires.com/figures-de-style/ellipse) discusses its opening; [Citation du Jour](https://citations.ouest-france.fr/idee/meilleurs-poemes-francais) names it individually. The latter list is not a global ranking and blocked one automated request, although a text fetch succeeded.
  - Lamartine's *Le Lac* is transcribed **in full**, 16 quatrains / 64 lines, from the unsigned original [1820 *Méditations poétiques*, pp. 46–49 / scan pp. 67–70](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Lamartine_-_Méditations_poétiques_(édition_de_1820).djvu?page=67), headed *Méditation dixième — Le Lac de B\*\*\**. [Études littéraires](https://www.etudes-litteraires.com/lamartine/le-lac) separately attributes and discusses the poem; its modernized text is not the source of this reading. The witness has `devoit`, `mugissois`, unaccented `O`, and `O temps ! suspends ton vol ; et vous, heures propices !`; repeated opening guillemets on each printed line of the quoted speech and indentation are normalized. The famous `O temps` stanzas are not counted independently.
  - Historical printings are public domain in the United States, not a determination of worldwide status. Copyright, edition-related and moral rights vary by jurisdiction; check local law. Scans are linked, not republished. The importer and reading snapshot (`scripts/import-recognized-world-classics-2.py` and `scripts/sources/recognized-world-classics-2-readings.txt`) identify checked copies by SHA-256; a hash does not prove transcription accuracy.

- Six individually documented historical works add **Du Mu**'s *江南春絶句*, **Zhu Xi**'s *春日*, **Ye Shaoweng**'s *遊園不值*, **Lin Sheng**'s *題臨安邸*, **Su Shi**'s *贈劉景文*, and **Robert Frost**'s *Mending Wall*. Each is counted once, not again for alternate titles, readings, translations, quotations or settings. The Hong Kong Education Bureau's individual recitation selections document regional attention, not a global popularity ranking or a license to copy modern commentary. The [Poetry Foundation guide](https://www.poetryfoundation.org/articles/150774/robert-frost-mending-wall) discusses *Mending Wall* individually and is likewise reception evidence, not the source of our transcription. No translations, recordings, inferred religious tags or composition-place claims are added.
  - *江南春絶句* follows the separately headed [*樊川文集*, 四部叢刊, page 7](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Sibu_Congkan0749-杜牧-樊川文集-5-2.djvu?page=7), immediately before *將赴宣州留題揚州禪智寺*. The linked facsimile reproduces a Ming printing; although the first 四部叢刊 series dates to 1919–22, the exact digitized impression has not been independently dated. The source text is ancient; the scan is linked, not republished.
  - The NLC-catalogued late-Ming *千家詩音釋* copy, dated 1621–1644 in its catalogue description, supplies individually headed quatrains on [PDF p. 2](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:NLC892-002340424-402613_千家詩音釋_二卷.pdf?page=2) (*春日*, attributed to 朱文公), [p. 6](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:NLC892-002340424-402613_千家詩音釋_二卷.pdf?page=6) (*遊園不值*), [p. 13](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:NLC892-002340424-402613_千家詩音釋_二卷.pdf?page=13) (*西湖*, attributed to 林夢屏), and [p. 15](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:NLC892-002340424-402613_千家詩音釋_二卷.pdf?page=15) (*冬景*, attributed to 蘇東坡). The latter two titles correspond respectively to the education selections *題臨安邸* and *贈劉景文*; 林夢屏 is identified there as Lin Sheng's courtesy name. The *西湖* witness reads `薰`, while the selection reads `熏`.
  - The late-Ming *遊園不值* reads `應嫌` / `十扣柴扉九不開` and names `葉清逸`. The separately inspected [*靖逸小集* in *兩宋名賢小集*, 卷二百六十, PDF pp. 21–25](https://archive.org/download/06048417.cn/06048417.cn.pdf#page=24) identifies 葉紹翁 as 靖逸 and reads `應憐` / `小扣柴扉久不開`; the education selection attributes the latter reading to Ye Shaoweng. The historical name discrepancy and textual differences remain disclosed rather than silently reconciled. The later reading is a search aid, not a second work.
  - *Mending Wall* follows the [first London edition of *North of Boston*, printed pp. 11–13 / PDF pp. 15–17](https://archive.org/download/northofboston00frosrich/northofboston00frosrich.pdf#page=15). The separately titled poem is complete in 45 lines after rejoining hanging printer wraps. The text retains `neighbour`, `neighbours`, `offence` and `out-door`; spacing and quotation typography are normalized. Publication in 1914 makes this printing public domain in the United States; Frost died in 1963, so this is **not** a claim of worldwide expiry. Copyright, edition-related and moral rights vary by jurisdiction; check local law.
  - For the Chinese poems, punctuation and verse line breaks are supplied, some historical glyph forms are normalized, and simplified-script/search aliases are not separate editions. These historical texts are public domain in the United States; local copyright and moral-rights rules vary. Wikimedia Commons marks the NLC and *樊川* scans public domain; the scanned images are linked, not republished. The checked source hashes and reproducible reading snapshot are in `scripts/import-recognized-multilingual-classics.py` and `scripts/sources/recognized-multilingual-classics-readings.txt`; hashes identify copies, not transcription accuracy.

- Four individually documented Song-dynasty poems introduce **Wang Anshi**'s *梅花*, *元日* and *登飛來峯*, and **Yang Wanli**'s *小池*. Each is one complete, separately headed work, not an additional variant, translation or musical setting. China is an associated place, not a claim about composition locations; the exact composition years are not asserted. No translations, recordings or inferred religious classifications are added.
  - *梅花* follows [*臨川文集*, 四庫全書 witness, 卷二十六, PDF p. 136](https://archive.org/download/06053552.cn/06053552.cn.pdf#page=136); the distinct *紅梅* follows it. *元日* follows [卷二十七, PDF pp. 13–14](https://archive.org/download/06053553.cn/06053553.cn.pdf#page=13): its final line crosses the page break and ends before *九日*. The main text reads `東風` and `爭插`, with an alternative to the latter in a small note; the [Hong Kong teaching selection](https://www.edb.gov.hk/attachment/tc/curriculum-development/kla/chi-edu/resources/primary/lang/jilei_shi_pdf/jilei_shi_074.pdf) reads `春風` and `總把`. The historical forms of the third line's dawn expression are normalized to `曈曈`; these textual or glyph variants are not counted separately.
  - *登飛來峯* follows [卷三十四, PDF p. 90](https://archive.org/download/06053554.cn/06053554.cn.pdf#page=90), ending before *讀漢功臣表*. This witness reads `飛來山上`, while the [Hong Kong teaching selection](https://www.edb.gov.hk/attachment/tc/curriculum-development/kla/chi-edu/resources/primary/lang/jilei_shi_pdf/jilei_shi_075.pdf) reads `飛來峰上` and discusses uncertainty about the geographical identification. `昇` is normalized to `升`; `峰` and simplified forms are search aliases, not separate works.
  - *小池* follows [*誠齋集*, 四庫全書薈要 witness, 卷七, PDF p. 70](https://archive.org/download/06074019.cn/06074019.cn.pdf#page=70), ending before *題左正卿壽慈堂*. Its `小荷纔露` differs in glyph from the [Hong Kong teaching selection](https://www.edb.gov.hk/attachment/tc/curriculum-development/kla/chi-edu/resources/primary/lang/jilei_shi_pdf/jilei_shi_085.pdf)'s `小荷才露`; `隂` is normalized to `陰`. This scan is from the *薈要* branch, not the separately transcribed *欽定四庫全書* copy.
  - The [Education Bureau](https://www.edb.gov.hk/attachment/tc/curriculum-development/kla/chi-edu/resources/primary/lang/jilei_shi_pdf/jilei_shi_073.pdf) also includes *梅花* individually in its primary recitation materials. [A 2022 selection](https://cul.china.com.cn/2022-07/04/content_42024451.htm) discusses *元日* and *登飛來峰*, and [CCTV](https://tv.cctv.com/special/zhsc/xcywl/index.shtml) presents *小池*. These sources document individual attention and educational or media reception, not a quantitative global ranking or permission to reproduce modern commentary or translations.
  - As a publication-history cross-check, separately inspected *四部叢刊* facsimiles show *梅花* at pages [92](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Sibu_Congkan0929-王安石-臨川先生文集-20-06.djvu?page=92)–[93](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Sibu_Congkan0929-王安石-臨川先生文集-20-06.djvu?page=93), *元日* at [103](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Sibu_Congkan0929-王安石-臨川先生文集-20-06.djvu?page=103), *登飛來峯* at [128](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Sibu_Congkan0930-王安石-臨川先生文集-20-07.djvu?page=128)–[129](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Sibu_Congkan0930-王安石-臨川先生文集-20-07.djvu?page=129), and *小池* at [99](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Sibu_Congkan1186-楊萬里-誠齋集-32-02.djvu?page=99). Both collections are listed in the [first series](https://zh.wikisource.org/wiki/四部叢刊), [published in 1919–22](https://epaper.gmw.cn/blqs/html/2015-10/01/nw.D110000blqs_20151001_3-02.htm); the exact impressions of these digitized copies are not established. The first three poems use a reproduction of a Ming printing and the fourth a manuscript copy, not the same witnesses as the linked Qing texts. These cross-checks do not establish authorial autographs or a first-publication year for each poem.
  - The historical poems and selected readings are public domain in the United States; copyright, edition-related and moral rights differ elsewhere, so check local law. Archive metadata identifies the Qing collection branches but not the particular manuscript or reproduction edition. Scans are linked, not republished. Verse was independently transcribed from the unpunctuated Qing witnesses with supplied line breaks and punctuation and normalized historical glyphs; no modern commentary is reproduced. `python3 scripts/import-recognized-song-classics.py --check` reproduces the [reading snapshot](../scripts/sources/recognized-song-classics-readings.txt); optional `--evidence-dir <directory>` verifies four PDF and six facsimile-image hashes.
