The [manifest](../scripts/sources/hafiz-virtues-1922.json) pins both scans, pages and text hashes; with the PDFs named as above, run `python3 scripts/import-hafiz-virtues-1922.py <evidence-directory> --check`.
- Seven Arabic poems by ʿĀʾisha Taymur follow the [*Ḥilyat al-ṭirāz* scan catalogued as 1303 AH](https://archive.org/details/AAlexandrina-149309). The archive gives 1886; [Marilyn Booth](https://accessingmuslimlives.org/profile/aisha-taymur/) gives 1303 AH as 1885–86 and records the Egyptian poet's 1840–1902 dates. Egypt is associated with the poet, not asserted as a composition place. The historical originals and printing are public domain in the United States; local law may differ. Wikisource contributors' transcriptions informed the readings and were adapted to the printing; contributors are credited under [CC BY-SA 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/), with the revision and contributor history linked on each reader page. No translations are supplied.
  - Each selected heading, text and following boundary was checked against the 1303 AH printing, and compared with Zaynab Fawwaz's separately typeset [*al-Durr al-manthūr*](https://archive.org/details/KitabAlDorAlManthorFeyTabaqatRabaatAlKhodoor). Its [title page](https://archive.org/download/KitabAlDorAlManthorFeyTabaqatRabaatAlKhodoor/KitabAlDorAlManthorFeyTabaqatRabaatAlKhodoor.pdf#page=15) identifies the first printing as 1312 AH (1894–95), despite a conflicting catalogue description. Both printings separately bound the selected poems with the same couplet counts; this establishes their boundaries in these printings, not every tradition, and does not prove independent textual descent. For these pages, printed page `p` is PDF page `p + 4` in the 1303 scan and `p + 40` in Fawwaz. A [second scan](https://archive.org/details/3518pdf) was used to clarify the same 1303 typesetting, not counted as a separate edition. The [review manifest](../scripts/sources/taymur-1303-reviewed.json) pins all three scans, pages, revisions and readings.
  - Material variants are disclosed on affected pages rather than blended: the 1303 `تجلى النور` reads `عن البشرى فأشرقت الليالي` and `والإقبال نالي`, while Fawwaz has `عن البشرى كإشراق الليالي` and `والإقبال عالي`. In `إن سال من غرب العيون بحور`, the 1303 `وتينها` corresponds to Fawwaz's `فؤادها`; `بنتاه يا كبدي ولوعة مهجتي` is an internal response in that elegy, not a separate poem. Both printings contain 50 couplets, including two omitted by Wikisource. The 1303 chronogram is followed by a printed 1294 AH annotation, which is not independently asserted as a composition date. `ملك الفؤاد وقد هجر` and `ملك الفؤاد وقد وشى` are separately headed, distinct works in both sources.
  - Vowel marks and ornamental spacing are omitted, spelling conventions normalized, and each printed half-line is shown on its own line except where a word is split. Headnotes are not part of the verse. The Hindawi digital anthology merges material from different poems and is not used as the authority for boundaries; its publication date for the book also conflicts with the scanned title page. Fawwaz gives only two couplets of `منشور حسنك في الحشا سطرته` before another heading, while the diwan continues the work; that excerpt is excluded. No religious affiliation or denomination is inferred from these poems or their author.
- Eight Arabic originals by Khalil Mutran follow dated magazine witnesses: the [June 1908 *al-Muqtabas* review](https://archive.org/download/1908_20240921_202409/%D9%85%D8%AC%D9%84%D8%A9%20%D8%A7%D9%84%D9%85%D9%82%D8%AA%D8%A8%D8%B3%201908.pdf#page=356) and [*al-Zuhūr*](https://archive.org/details/ar411zouh01) issues from 1910–13. The scanned issue headings date the [1908 review](https://archive.org/download/1908_20240921_202409/%D9%85%D8%AC%D9%84%D8%A9%20%D8%A7%D9%84%D9%85%D9%82%D8%AA%D8%A8%D8%B3%201908.pdf#page=301), the [April 1910 flowers poem](https://archive.org/download/ar411zouh01/%D9%85%D8%AC%D9%84%D8%A9%20%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%B2%D9%87%D9%88%D8%B1%20%D9%85%D8%B5%D8%B1%20%D8%B3%D9%86%D8%A9%201910.pdf#page=45), and the [March 1912 elegies](https://archive.org/download/ar411zouh01/%D9%85%D8%AC%D9%84%D8%A9%20%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%B2%D9%87%D9%88%D8%B1%20%D9%85%D8%B5%D8%B1%20%D8%B3%D9%86%D8%A9%201912.pdf#page=7). The second and third bound years extend to February 1912 and February 1913 respectively; the year in a scan filename is not assumed to date every issue. The [BnF authority](https://catalogue.bnf.fr/ark:/12148/cb11288236s) records the poet's 1872–1949 dates, Baalbek birth and life in Cairo. Lebanon and Egypt are poet associations, not asserted composition places. The originals as published before 1931 are public domain in the United States; check local law.
  - The 1908 review presents Baalbek as a sample. Its 59 couplets begin and end with the separately headed poem in the [undated al-Maʿārif dīwān](https://archive.org/details/AAlexandrina-143009); this comparison establishes the boundaries in these printings, not in all traditions or independent textual descent. The archive catalogues this book as `[1928]`, but the scan does not establish when all its contents first appeared. A [separately typeset 1949 volume](https://archive.org/download/1_20250825_20250825_2351/%D8%AF%D9%8A%D9%88%D8%A7%D9%86%20%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%AE%D9%84%D9%8A%D9%84%20%D9%84%D9%84%D8%B4%D8%A7%D8%B9%D8%B1%20%D8%AE%D9%84%D9%8A%D9%84%20%D9%85%D8%B7%D8%B1%D8%A7%D9%86%20%D9%80%201%20%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%AC%D8%B2%D8%A1%20%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A3%D9%88%D9%84.pdf#page=98) revises the childhood butterfly couplet. The 1908 `الليال` differs from the undated book's `الليالي`; the magazine reading is retained. The 1949 volume is a comparison, not the source of a presumed public-domain modern edition.
  - `الزهرات الثلاث` is one signed 22-couplet work, not three separately counted poems; the [later volume 2](https://archive.org/details/2_20250825_20250825_2353) calls it `القاضي العادل` and prints `تاج قطن` where the 1910 magazine has `تاج عهن`. The magazine introduces the 16-couplet Saba elegy and then separates the 12-couplet Zurayq elegy with prose; the later volume separately heads them. It omits the magazine's `ماد نسيم به فمات وفي / معطفه رقة من الميد` and reads `شاركتك` where the magazine has `ساهمتك`. The two versions are not spliced and the reason for the omission is unknown. The two-couplet `قطرة دم` and `دموع الحبيب` have separate magazine headings and signatures and are also separated in [volume 3](https://archive.org/details/3_20250825_20250825_2355). The magazine separately presents the Musset-volume inscription; a [1977 collected edition](https://archive.org/download/BBib-Alex-02946/%D8%AF%D9%8A%D9%88%D8%A7%D9%86_%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%AE%D9%84%D9%8A%D9%84_03_02946_%D9%85%D8%B7%D8%B1%D8%A7%D9%86%D8%8C_%D8%AE%D9%84%D9%8A%D9%84%D8%8C.pdf#page=521) separately prints the same eight couplets, with `دمع عينيه` and `ذلك القلب` where the magazine has `دمع عينه` and `ذاك القلب`. Its [copyright page](https://archive.org/download/BBib-Alex-02946/%D8%AF%D9%8A%D9%88%D8%A7%D9%86_%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%AE%D9%84%D9%8A%D9%84_03_02946_%D9%85%D8%B7%D8%B1%D8%A7%D9%86%D8%8C_%D8%AE%D9%84%D9%8A%D9%84%D8%8C.pdf#page=8) reserves all rights; it is linked for comparison, not reproduced or treated as permission to copy that edition. `الفل` is separately headed and signed in the magazine; its wider textual history is not established. These comparisons do not claim independent descent or completeness beyond the named printings.
  - The [review manifest](../scripts/sources/mutran-magazines-reviewed.json) pins the nine scans, printed and PDF pages, and consulted Wikisource revisions; the [reading file](../scripts/sources/mutran-magazines-reviewed.txt) records the magazine texts. Six readings were informed by Wikisource contributor transcriptions and adapted to the historical scans; contributors are credited under [CC BY-SA 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/) with revision histories in the corresponding rights notes. Several consulted Wikisource pages misattribute these poems to Kahlil Gibran. The flowers and `الفل` texts were transcribed from the magazine. Vowel marks and ornamental dividers are omitted, spelling and spacing normalized, and printed half-lines placed on separate lines except where a word crosses the break; these are reading texts, not diplomatic facsimiles. Scans are linked, not republished. No translations, composition places, exact composition dates, or religious classifications are supplied.
- Six Arabic originals by Ismail Sabri follow the dated [*al-Zuhūr*](https://archive.org/details/ar411zouh01) magazine scans, with separate comparisons to the [1938 *Dīwān Ismāʿīl Ṣabrī Bāshā*](https://archive.org/details/3235pdf1357). Its [title page](https://archive.org/download/3235pdf1357/3235%20%D9%83%D8%AA%D8%A7%D8%A8%20%D8%A7%D9%82%D8%B1%D8%A7%20%D8%A7%D9%88%D9%86%D9%84%D8%A7%D9%8A%D9%86%20%20%20%20pdf%20%20%20%D8%AF%D9%8A%D9%88%D8%A7%D9%86%20%D8%A5%D8%B3%D9%85%D8%A7%D8%B9%D9%8A%D9%84%20%D8%B5%D8%A8%D8%B1%D9%8A%20%D8%A8%D8%A7%D8%B4%D8%A7%20-%20%D8%B7%201357.pdf#page=2) identifies its date and editor. The book is linked to compare boundaries and variants, not reproduced or treated as permission to reuse its editorial work. The [BnF authority](https://catalogue.bnf.fr/ark:/12148/cb16183583f) identifies Sabri as an Egyptian poet and magistrate, 1854–1923. Egypt is an author association, not an asserted composition place. The magazine originals published before 1931 are public domain in the United States; check local law.
  - The [April 1910 issue heading](https://archive.org/download/ar411zouh01/%D9%85%D8%AC%D9%84%D8%A9%20%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%B2%D9%87%D9%88%D8%B1%20%D9%85%D8%B5%D8%B1%20%D8%B3%D9%86%D8%A9%201910.pdf#page=45) dates `دمعة`; the [February 1911 heading](https://archive.org/download/ar411zouh01/%D9%85%D8%AC%D9%84%D8%A9%20%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%B2%D9%87%D9%88%D8%B1%20%D9%85%D8%B5%D8%B1%20%D8%B3%D9%86%D8%A9%201910.pdf#page=517) dates `عبد بلا ثمن`. The first bound year runs through February 1911, so its later pages are not all assumed to be 1910. The [February 1912 issue](https://archive.org/download/ar411zouh01/%D9%85%D8%AC%D9%84%D8%A9%20%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%B2%D9%87%D9%88%D8%B1%20%D9%85%D8%B5%D8%B1%20%D8%B3%D9%86%D8%A9%201911.pdf#page=509) contains `إلى سابا باشا`, distinct from Mutran's elegy for the same family. The [October 1913 heading](https://archive.org/download/ar411zouh01/%D9%85%D8%AC%D9%84%D8%A9%20%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%B2%D9%87%D9%88%D8%B1%20%D9%85%D8%B5%D8%B1%20%D8%B3%D9%86%D8%A9%201913.pdf#page=285) dates the Tusun poem. Publication dates do not establish composition dates.
  - Each selected magazine text has the same opening, final words and couplet count as a separately bounded poem in the later dīwān; this supports completeness in those printings, not every tradition or independent textual descent. In `دمعة`, the magazine has `ويحفظ العهد كما شاءه` where the book has `ويأخذ البر وآي الوفا`; neither reading is silently substituted. In `بكاء صديق`, the magazine's `تقاص منك ظل` differs from the book's `تقلص منك ظل`; in `عبد بلا ثمن`, `من لؤلؤ` and `في أفقه` differ from `عن لؤلؤ` and `في أوجه`. `أقريب من دنف غده` is Sabri's separately signed response to an older poem, not a translation or one of the other poets' responses printed alongside it. The Tusun poem ends before the later book's prose reply.
  - The [review manifest](../scripts/sources/sabri-magazines-reviewed.json) pins all four scans and printed/PDF pages; the [reading file](../scripts/sources/sabri-magazines-reviewed.txt) transcribes the magazine, omitting vowel marks and ornamental dividers and normalizing spelling and spacing. Half-lines are separated except where a word crosses the break. These are reading texts, not diplomatic facsimiles. `ساعة الوداع` is excluded: the magazine has four couplets, whereas the separately headed later book version has eight, including an earlier opening. `يوم الفراق` is explicitly labeled the beginning of a poem in the magazine and is excluded. Neither difference establishes why the versions differ. No translations, religious affiliations or devotional classifications are inferred.

Wikisource pages may include transcriptions and contributions under their stated Creative Commons terms; follow each linked page and revision history for attribution and license details. The MIT license in this repository applies to application code, not automatically to third-party content. Images and fonts have separate terms; the fonts' SIL OFL texts are in `assets/fonts/`.

## Expanded historical collections

- [*Three Hundred Tang Poems*, Gutenberg 52323](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/52323): 319 selections from the numbered Chinese transcription; no. 233 is omitted because Li Bai's *Quiet Night Thought* already has a stable page. The eBook does not identify a particular print edition. Names follow the source, with readable romanizations; the source's 劉春虛 is associated with [Liu Shenxu, 劉眘虛 / 劉慎虛](https://zh.wikisource.org/wiki/%E9%97%95%E9%A1%8C_(%E5%8A%89%E7%9C%98%E8%99%9B)). 西鄙人 is an anonymous designation, not an identified person. Dates and precise creation places are not inferred.
- [*Las cien mejores poesías de la lengua castellana*, Gutenberg 65880](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/65880): the 1910 third edition selected by Marcelino Menéndez y Pelayo. It includes traditional romances and Spanish-language poets from outside Spain; the anthology's own author headings are recorded, including anonymous attributions. Section and speaker headings and historical spelling are retained.
- [*The Book of American Negro Poetry*, Gutenberg 11986](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/11986): James Weldon Johnson's 1922 collection, with its historical spellings and dialect retained. Some texts reflect the racism and violence of their period. Explicitly marked excerpts and ambiguous nested selections are excluded; prose-poetry paragraphs are preserved alongside verse.
- Associated-place review for `castellana-072`: [Universidad de Chile's Andrés Bello biography](https://uchile.cl/presentacion/historia/rectores-de-la-u-de-chile/andres-bello-lopez-1843-1865) documents his dates (1781–1865), Caracas origins, literary work during his London years (1810–1829), and career in Chile. Venezuela, England and Chile describe the poet's associations, not an asserted composition location; the date of the poem is left unestablished here.
- Associated-place review for `american-1922-00633`: the [Library of Virginia's Robert Nathaniel Dett biography](https://www.lva.virginia.gov/collections/dvb/bio/dett-r-nathaniel) documents his dates (1882–1943), Ontario birth and United States career. The [Academy of American Poets](https://poets.org/poem/rubinstein-staccato-etude) also credits the poem to him. Canada and the United States describe the poet's associations, not an asserted composition location; the date of the poem is left unestablished here.
- [*Livro de Mágoas*, Gutenberg 17610](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/17610): Florbela Espanca's 1919 collection, including the historical spelling of the transcription.
- Three further individually headed Bilac sonnets follow the public-domain Princeton/HathiTrust copy of *Tarde* (1919): [*Fogo fatuo*, pp. 126–127](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Tarde_(1919).djvu?page=130), [*Remorso*, pp. 130–131](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Tarde_(1919).djvu?page=134), and [*Symphonia*, pp. 206–207](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Tarde_(1919).djvu?page=210). Every line and boundary was also checked against the USP copy, whose noncommercial, attribution and online-republication notice is described below; neither scan image is reproduced. These copies are of the same printing, not independent editions. The readings use historical wording with some typographic normalization and are not diplomatic editions or proof of a final author-approved text. No translation, composition date, place of composition, or faith classification is inferred.
  - Comparison aids were [Portal São Francisco](https://www.portalsaofrancisco.com.br/obras-literarias/fogo-fatuo-olavo-bilac) for *Fogo fatuo*, [Escritas.org](https://www.escritas.org/pt/t/4899/remorso) and [Recanto das Letras](https://www.recantodasletras.com.br/poesias-de-desilusao/7729174) for *Remorso*, and [Travessia Poética](https://valiteratura.blogspot.com/2023/02/sinfonia-olavo-bilac.html) for *Symphonia*. The 1919 *Remorso* has `esperdicei` and `calando`; Escritas has `desperdicei`, while the Recanto posting gives `calado` and cites a 1970 edition that was not independently checked. Recanto marks its posting [CC BY-NC 2.5 Brazil](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-nc/2.5/br/); it is a comparison aid, not the historical text or rights basis. With the two files named as described below, `python3 scripts/import-bilac-tarde-more-1919.py <evidence-directory> --check` reproduces the readings. The [manifest](../scripts/sources/bilac-tarde-more-1919.json) and [reading file](../scripts/sources/bilac-tarde-more-1919.txt) pin the scans, page mappings and texts.
- Olavo Bilac, *Tarde* (Rio de Janeiro: Francisco Alves, 1919): five individually headed complete sonnets follow the Princeton/HathiTrust copy on Wikimedia Commons, with the aid of modern comparison transcriptions: [*Cyclo*, pp. 12–13](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Tarde_(1919).djvu?page=16), [*Patria*, pp. 14–15](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Tarde_(1919).djvu?page=18), [*Lingua Portugueza*, pp. 16–17](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Tarde_(1919).djvu?page=20), [*A um poeta*, pp. 118–119](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Tarde_(1919).djvu?page=122), and [*Aos sinos*, pp. 204–205](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Tarde_(1919).djvu?page=208). Commons marks its mechanical scan public domain. USP's [catalogue](https://digital.bbm.usp.br/handle/bbm/4541) identifies the 1919 volume and public-domain status; the [Academia Brasileira de Letras biography](https://www.academia.org.br/academicos/olavo-bilac/biografia) records Bilac's 1865–1918 dates and Brazilian career. The pre-1931 originals are public domain in the United States; check local law. No translation, composition date or place, religious denomination, or devotional classification is supplied.
  - Every selected line and boundary was checked against the [USP comparison scan](https://digital.bbm.usp.br/bitstream/bbm/4541/1/002927_COMPLETO.pdf); these are copies of the same printing, not independent editions or manuscript witnesses. The [USP scan's usage notice](https://digital.bbm.usp.br/bitstream/bbm/4541/1/002927_COMPLETO.pdf#page=221) requests noncommercial use and attribution, prohibits commercial use of its images, and asks that its digital document not be republished online without authorization. The USP scan is linked for comparison, not reproduced. It lacks printed pp. 124–125 and 128–129, so its leaf offset changes; the selected pages are intact and their mappings are explicit in the [manifest](../scripts/sources/bilac-tarde-1919.json). *Cyclo* is one sonnet, not four works. The text is a reading transcription, not a diplomatic edition or a claim that the posthumous printing represents a final author-approved text. Spacing, punctuation, some diacritics and apostrophes are normalized. A damaged mark over the `e` in *A um poeta*'s `socego` is omitted and disclosed, rather than asserted as certain.
  - The [Academia Brasileira de Letras selected texts](https://www.academia.org.br/academicos/olavo-bilac/textos-escolhidos) compare *Lingua Portugueza*, *A um poeta* and *Aos sinos*: the 1919 `de oceano largo`, `na paciencia` and `mais vasta` differ from the ABL's `de oceanos largos`, `no silêncio` and `mais casta`. ABL calls the last *Os sinos*; both titles are searchable. The 1919 readings are retained, not silently blended. Modern comparisons were consulted, not reproduced as historical editions: [Templo Cultural Delfos](https://www.elfikurten.com.br/2012/06/olavo-bilac-o-principe-dos-poetas.html) for *Cyclo*; [Pensador](https://www.pensador.com/frase/MTkxODk5Ng) and [Wikisource](https://pt.wikisource.org/w/index.php?title=P%C3%A1tria_(Olavo_Bilac)&oldid=35276) for *Patria*; and [Wikisource's scan-backed *A um poeta*](https://pt.wikisource.org/w/index.php?title=Tarde_(1919)A_um_poeta&oldid=472204) for the latter sonnet. Wikisource contributors' transcriptions are under [CC BY-SA 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/); consult the linked article and scan-page histories. To reproduce the readings, save the USP PDF as `tarde-1919.pdf` and the Commons DjVu as `tarde-hathitrust-1919.djvu`, then run `python3 scripts/import-bilac-tarde-1919.py <evidence-directory> --check`. The manifest and [reading file](../scripts/sources/bilac-tarde-1919.txt) pin both scans, page mappings and output hashes.
- Gonçalves Dias, *Cantos*, third edition (Leipzig: Brockhaus, 1860): four Brazilian Portuguese originals follow [Gutenberg 74854](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/74854), compared with the UFSC-hosted scan: [*Canção do Exilio*, pp. 3–4](https://literaturabrasileira.ufsc.br/midias/stream?id=117524&raw=1#page=31), [*Cachias*, pp. 11–12](https://literaturabrasileira.ufsc.br/midias/stream?id=117524&raw=1#page=39), [*Desejo*, p. 19](https://literaturabrasileira.ufsc.br/midias/stream?id=117524&raw=1#page=47), and [*Te Deum*, pp. 109–110](https://literaturabrasileira.ufsc.br/midias/stream?id=117524&raw=1#page=137). The texts retain the edition's spellings; the stanza in *Cachias* continues across a page break, rather than being divided there as in the Gutenberg transcription. The separately printed epigraphs are omitted from the poem text and acknowledged in the relevant reader contexts. The edition dates *Canção do Exilio* Coimbra, July 1843; other composition dates and places are not established here. The [Academia Brasileira de Letras biography](https://www.academia.org.br/academicos/goncalves-dias/biografia) documents the poet's Caxias, Maranhão birth and Brazilian career. *Te Deum* is a hymn of praise; its title recalls a [Christian hymn used in Catholic and Anglican traditions](https://www.encyclopedia.com/philosophy-and-religion/christianity/roman-catholic-and-orthodox-churches-liturgy-hymns-and-prayers/te-deum-laudamus), not evidence of the poet's denomination. No translations are supplied.
  - Fifteen further separately headed works follow the same [1860 Gutenberg transcription](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/74854), checked against OCR and selected pages of the [UFSC-hosted 1860 scan](https://literaturabrasileira.ufsc.br/midias/stream?id=117524&raw=1). Gutenberg credits Rita Farinha, Laura Natal, Alberto Manuel Brandão Simões and the Online Distributed Proofreading Team, identifies Google Books images and marks the eBook public domain in the United States. Its [permission guidance](https://www.gutenberg.org/policy/permission.html#crediting) permits linked source credits, and its [license explanation](https://www.gutenberg.org/policy/license.html#using-the-project-gutenberg-trademark) distinguishes such references from trademark branding. These records cite the source rather than present themselves as a Gutenberg eBook. The UFSC scan is also Google-digitized: both represent the same printing, not independent historical witnesses, and whether they use different copies is not established. The originals and 1860 printing are public domain in the United States; check local law. The scans are linked, not republished; no translations are supplied.
  - The selected verse sequences and boundaries were compared with the 1860 scan's OCR and separately printed [1846 *Primeiros cantos*](https://digital.bbm.usp.br/handle/bbm/4135) ([PDF](https://digital.bbm.usp.br/bitstream/bbm/4135/1/006342_COMPLETO.pdf)) or [1851 *Ultimos cantos*](https://digital.bbm.usp.br/handle/bbm/4123) ([PDF](https://digital.bbm.usp.br/bitstream/bbm/4123/1/006346_COMPLETO.pdf)), using scan-backed Wikisource transcriptions as comparison aids. Most selected 1851 transclusion pages are unproofread; the four selected 1846 transcriptions and *Marabá*'s 1851 pages are proofread, not independently validated. Selected differences, headings and page boundaries were checked visually. This is OCR-assisted collation, not a diplomatic, character-by-character or exhaustive comparison; separately printed editions are not proof of independent manuscript descent. Completeness refers to the selected 1860 printing. The [manifest](../scripts/sources/goncalves-dias-cantos-1860.json) records the earlier PDF leaves explicitly: their printed-page offsets differ from the 1860 scan.
  - *O Canto do Guerreiro*, *O Canto do Piaga* and *Canção do Tamoyo* retain their nine, three and ten numbered internal sections as one work each. Their headings count as nonblank lines in Verso; section numbers and the four separately numbered 1860 *Poesias Americanas* titles do not become extra works. *Marabá*'s last passage and *Cumprimento de um voto*'s three passages likewise remain within their respective works. Indentation and typographic rules are omitted, spacing and some punctuation normalized, and Gutenberg's stanza layout retained. *Marabá* retains Gutenberg's dialogue punctuation, including closing quotation marks absent from the 1860 scan. Printed epigraphs attributed to A. de Gusmão, Chénier and `CAM., Rim.` are omitted from the corresponding verse; separate explanatory endnotes are not counted as poems. The historical depictions of Indigenous people are not represented as documented Indigenous testimony.
  - Four source corrections follow the 1860 scan rather than silently blending editions: *Piaga* restores `Seos estridulos` from Gutenberg's `Seus estridulos`; *Marabá* restores `Qual d’uma palmeira` from `Qual duma palmeira`; *Tamoyo* restores `Das setas ligeiras` from `Das seta ligeiras`; and *Sobre o tumulo de um menino* restores `offrenda` from `off’renda`. The scan's unusual `Robuste, fragueiro` in *Tamoyo* and `Que faces no teu scismar?` in *A concha e a virgem* are retained, while the 1851 printing reads `Robusto` and `fazes`. Other scan-confirmed changes between editions, including *Guerreiro*'s `estimão`/`estimem`, *Piaga*'s reordered moon line, *Indio*'s changed line about seeing the beloved, *Cumprimento*'s `voz`/`vós`, *Urge o tempo*'s `não muda`/`nem muda`, and *Menina e moça*'s `inceta`/`enceta`, are disclosed in the readers. These examples are not an exhaustive variant census.
  - The printed `25 de Outubro de 1848`, the heading `Porto das Caixas—25 de agosto 1850`, and `28 de Julho` without a year are described in context, not treated as independent proof of composition dates or places. The [1851 endnote to *Que me pedes*](https://digital.bbm.usp.br/bitstream/bbm/4123/1/006346_COMPLETO.pdf#page=311) explicitly dates the composition 9 March 1849; that sourced date is recorded, without asserting a place. To reproduce the records, select “Read online now” on the [Gutenberg eBook page](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/74854) and save the HTML as `gutenberg-74854.html`; save the 1860 UFSC PDF and the 1846 and 1851 PDFs as `cantos-1860-ufsc.pdf`, `primeiros-cantos-1846.pdf` and `ultimos-cantos-1851.pdf`. Run `uv run --no-project --with beautifulsoup4 python scripts/import-goncalves-dias-1860.py <evidence-directory> --check`. The importer pins source hashes, section headings, page ranges, corrections and output hashes. The original four Dias records and their URLs remain unchanged.
- Machado de Assis: 23 distinct Brazilian Portuguese originals follow scan-backed Wikisource transcriptions: 16 from the [1902 *Poesias Completas*](https://pt.wikisource.org/wiki/Poesias_Completas_%28Machado_de_Assis%29) and seven from [*Chrysalidas* (1864)](https://pt.wikisource.org/wiki/Chrysalidas_%281864%29). The [Academia Brasileira de Letras biography](https://www.academia.org.br/academicos/machado-de-assis/biografia) documents Machado's dates (1839–1908), Rio de Janeiro birth and Brazilian career. Both editions are public domain in the United States; Wikisource's transcription and contributors are credited under [CC BY-SA 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/). Each article is linked at a recorded revision; consult article and transcluded scan-page histories for contributors. The 1902 pages used here were singly proofread, not independently validated; the selected 1864 pages were validated. A proofread status does not guarantee a perfect text.
  - We compared every 1902 verse line to the independently transcribed [1901 edition, Gutenberg 61653](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/61653), ignoring case, accents and punctuation but retaining word boundaries. The two editions and their punctuation are not asserted to be identical. Five of the seven 1864 texts were compared line by line to the 1901 transcription; differences are recorded in the [manifest](../scripts/sources/machado-reviewed.json) and checked against the 1864 scans. *Fé* and *A Caridade* were compared with independently transcribed modern-spelling texts at [Citador](https://www.citador.pt/poemas/fe-joaquim-maria-machado-de-assis) and [Citador](https://www.citador.pt/poemas/a-caridade-joaquim-maria-machado-de-assis), respectively, and with their entire 1864 scan pages. Citador is a comparison, not the source of the reproduced historical text. Gutenberg also contains apparent transcription errors; do not copy it blindly.
  - Four transcription corrections follow the 1864 scans: *Visio*, line 6 `Entro` → [`Entre`](https://pt.wikisource.org/wiki/P%C3%A1gina:Chrysalidas.pdf/37) and line 41 `Como olhar` → [`Com o olhar`](https://pt.wikisource.org/wiki/P%C3%A1gina:Chrysalidas.pdf/39); *Erro*, line 21 `Quo` → [`Que`](https://pt.wikisource.org/wiki/P%C3%A1gina:Chrysalidas.pdf/60); and *A Caridade*, line 18 → [`Dando-lhes leito e pão, guarida e amor.`](https://pt.wikisource.org/wiki/P%C3%A1gina:Chrysalidas.pdf/44). The 1864 *Erro* retains `Nullo` where the 1901 edition reads `Morto`; *Musa consolatrix* retains `terá` where the later transcription has `haverá`. *Horas vivas* retains the scan's `explendores`. We restored stanza breaks lost at page boundaries in both editions, and the break after line 4 of [*Fé*](https://pt.wikisource.org/wiki/P%C3%A1gina:Chrysalidas.pdf/41); *A Caridade* continues across its page break. The manifest pins source and output hashes, comparison differences, corrections and restored breaks.
  - *Lindoya*'s italicized final line adapts a line from [Basílio da Gama's *O Uraguai*](https://www.academia.org.br/academicos/basilio-da-gama/textos-escolhidos); it is rendered as plain text. Separately printed epigraphs in *Stella*, *Erro* and *Fé*, the dedication in *Horas vivas*, and printed dates are not included in the verse. The 1864 edition attributes *Fé*'s Spanish epigraph to Santa Thereza de Jesus; its authorship is disputed. *Fé* is a prayerful poem, not proof of the poet's denomination. *José de Anchieta* concerns a Christian historical figure, and *Soneto de Natal* is set at Christmas; neither is automatically classified as devotional. Translations, the French-language piece and uncertain fragments are not counted as original Portuguese works; no translations are supplied here.
- Auta de Souza, *Horto*, second edition (1910): seven complete Brazilian Portuguese works follow the [1910 scan](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Horto_(1910).djvu), marked public domain by Commons. The poems and pre-1931 edition are public domain in the United States; Wikisource's transcriptions are credited under [CC BY-SA 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/). Consult each linked article and transcluded page history for contributors, and check local law. The [UFSC-hosted catalogue biography](https://catalogodeescritoras.ufsc.br/catalogo/auta_vida.html), organized by Constância Lima Duarte and Diva Cunha, records the poet's Macaíba birth, 1876–1901 dates and the 1910 second edition. Brazil is associated with the poet, not asserted as a composition location.
  - Each poem's complete wording and boundaries were checked manually against the printed pages; the [manifest](../scripts/sources/auta-horto-reviewed.json) pins the scan images, article revisions, text and line-by-line comparisons. *Cantiga*'s Wikisource scan page is validated; *Caminho do sertão*, *Nunca mais*, *Bohemias* and *Regina Cœli* have singly proofread pages; *Supplica* and *Fio Partido* are unreviewed and modernize the print. For *Caminho*, scan 105 is an illustrated title plate, 106 is blank and [107](https://pt.wikisource.org/wiki/P%C3%A1gina:Horto_(1910).djvu/107) contains the complete sonnet. In *Nunca mais*, *Bohemias*, *Regina Cœli* and *Fio Partido*, stanza boundaries have been supplied at page breaks; the two *Regina Cœli* quatrains at the second boundary are an editorial layout choice based on the surrounding pattern.
  - The [*Supplica* scan](https://pt.wikisource.org/wiki/P%C3%A1gina:Horto_(1910).djvu/275) prints `desparece`, whereas both Wikisource and [Escritas.org](https://www.escritas.org/pt/t/12697/suplica) have `desaparece`; the print is retained, along with its punctuation and historical spelling. The [*Bohemias* scan](https://pt.wikisource.org/wiki/P%C3%A1gina:Horto_(1910).djvu/140) has `tambem` where Wikisource has `também`. Its [later comparison](https://www.escritas.org/pt/t/12693/boemias) contains only five of the eight 1910 stanzas and reads `desabrochar` and `chorasse`, where the print has `desbrochar` and `chorasses`. The later *Fio Partido* comparison reads `na amplidão` where the print has `n’amplidão`; *Nunca mais* also differs in punctuation and spelling. The later pages identify a fourth edition but give an incomplete publication date. They are comparisons only, not sources or permissions to copy their modern texts; their author biography is internally inconsistent and is not used as biographical evidence.
  - Printed dedications, epigraphs, dates and the dotted rules in *Cantiga* are not part of the displayed verse; *Fio Partido*'s printed section II is retained. Indentation and spaces around punctuation are normalized; this is a reading transcription, not a facsimile. No unreviewed translations, later purported psychographed works, or exact composition dates or places are supplied. Faith classification follows the content of individual works, not an inference about the poet's denomination. *Oração da noite* and *N’um leque* are excluded pending resolution of unclear scan readings.
- Castro Alves, *Espumas fluctuantes*: nine complete Brazilian Portuguese poems follow the validated [Wikisource transcription](https://pt.wikisource.org/wiki/Espumas_Flutuantes_(1913)) of a [Garnier scan](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Espumas_fluctuantes_(corr._e_augm.).djvu). The title page has no printed date: Internet Archive and [UFSC's catalogue, citing Internet Archive](https://literaturabrasileira.ufsc.br/documentos?id=221666), give 1913, while Commons says **1913?**; that dating is not presented as certain. The same works appear in the [1870 first edition](https://literaturabrasileira.ufsc.br/documentos?id=132499), which is linked at the relevant scan page for comparison. The [Academia Brasileira de Letras](https://www.academia.org.br/academicos/castro-alves/biografia) confirms the author's 1847–1871 dates, Brazilian associations, and 1870 first publication. The historical poems and early edition are public domain in the United States; Commons marks the scan public domain. The Wikisource text and its contributors are credited under [CC BY-SA 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/); consult article and transcluded scan-page histories, and check local law. No translations are supplied.
  - Each selected Garnier scan page was checked against its transcribed verse. The 1870 scan and its OCR were consulted for boundaries and selected variants, not treated as a full critical collation. In *Hebréa*, Wikisource's `Nào` is corrected to the scan's `Não`; in *O laço de fita*, `voaras` and `c′ròa` are corrected to `voavas` and `c′rôa`; in *A volta da primavera*, `Respondo`, `peneira`, and `foz` are corrected to `Responde`, `penetra`, and `fez`; and in *A Maciel Pinheiro*, the joined `lyrae` is separated to `lyra e`. A punctuation correction in the last poem also follows the scan. The [manifest](../scripts/sources/castro-alves-reviewed.json) pins source revisions, transcluded text hashes, scan hashes, corrections, page ranges, restored stanza breaks, and output hashes.
  - Historical edition differences are not silently harmonized: the Garnier *O «Adeus» de Thereza* prints `Era a a pallida Thereza` where the [1870 first edition](https://literaturabrasileira.ufsc.br/midias/stream?id=114609&raw=1#page=63) prints one `a`; the Garnier *O fantasma e a canção* reverses two dialogue lines relative to the [1870 first edition](https://literaturabrasileira.ufsc.br/midias/stream?id=114609&raw=1#page=48). These Garnier readings are retained and noted on the individual pages. Printed epigraphs, dedications and dates are omitted; apostrophes, punctuation, spacing and indentation follow the reading transcription rather than a diplomatic facsimile. Section numbers in *Os tres amores* remain within that single work. No exact composition place or date, religious denomination, or devotional classification is inferred.
- Francisca Júlia, *Mármores* (1895): 24 complete Brazilian Portuguese works follow [Wikisource's scan-transcluded first edition](https://pt.wikisource.org/wiki/Marmores_(1895)); each linked article is recorded at a revision. The [USP catalogue](https://digital.bbm.usp.br/handle/bbm/4488) identifies the 1895 first edition and marks the work public domain. The historical poems and pre-1931 edition are public domain in the United States; Wikisource's transcription and contributors are credited under [CC BY-SA 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/). Consult the article and transcluded page histories for contributors, and check local law. The [Commons copy](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Marmores.pdf) is linked for page-level inspection, not redistributed here. Commons labels the scan public domain, while USP's PDF states non-commercial-use terms for its digitized document and prohibits commercial use of its images; do not assume its scan has the same reuse terms as the historical text.
  - The poet's birth year is disputed: the [2026 Senate edition](https://www2.senado.leg.br/bdsf/handle/id/757594) adopts 1871 on the authority of her brother and notes that the 1895 preface gives 1874; the USP catalogue likewise gives 1874, although [USP's author essay](https://www.bbm.usp.br/pt-br/Selecao-BBM-digital/francisca-j%C3%BAlia-1871-1920) gives 1871. We display `1871?–1920` and disclose the disagreement. Brazil is associated with the poet, not asserted as a composition place; individual composition dates are not inferred. The later Senate edition modernizes the spelling and is not substituted for the 1895 reading.
  - Each selection's verse and boundaries were manually checked against the first-edition scans. Wikisource marks both *No boudoir* pages as unproofread and the closing *Musa impassivel* pages as one proofread and one unproofread; the others have varying proofread or validated statuses. A validation status does not guarantee an error-free text. Eighteen are the separately numbered opening sonnets; six are individually numbered works in *Ballada*. The opening and closing *Musa impassivel* are different sonnets, as are *A Noite* and *Á noite*. The seven Goethe/Heine translations are excluded from the count and not supplied as translations. *Estella* is excluded: the [scan of its first page](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Marmores.pdf?page=99) cuts off the beginnings of eight lines, despite the Wikisource page's validation status. The remaining *Ballada* works were not reviewed for this slice.
  - The scanned print reads `escameo`, not Wikisource's `escarneo`, in *Rainha das aguas*; `emquanto`, not `emquato`, in *Á noite*; `Terras extranhas`, not `Terra extranhas`, in *Ballada*; and `joven`, `A um canto` and `Entra`, not `jovem`, `A canto` and `Entre`, in *No boudoir*. A few punctuation corrections also follow the scan. In the closing *Musa impassivel*, a mark partially obscures `dos` on p. 100; Wikisource's reading is retained and the uncertainty noted. The [manifest](../scripts/sources/francisca-julia-marmores-reviewed.json) records all changes, original and restored stanza breaks, source hashes, proofreading status and PDF pages. Most page-break stanza boundaries are clear from the printed layout; in *De joelhos*, the break between pp. 66–67 is shown as two quatrains following the surrounding pattern, but the page turn alone does not establish that division. Indentation, apostrophes and some typography follow the reading transcription rather than a facsimile. Printed dedications are omitted from the verse and noted on the affected pages. *De joelhos* depicts a prayer to Christ and is classified by that poem's content, not an inferred denomination.
  - In *Sonho africano*, the [1895 print](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Marmores.pdf?page=48) reads `Pensa na longe patria` and `negros pituns`. A separately linked [modern-spelling transcription](https://www.escritas.org/pt/t/12343/sonho-africano) reads `Pensa na pátria, além` and `negros reptis`; its citation to *Poesias* gives an incomplete year, so it is a comparison rather than proof of a particular edition or a source of the 1895 reading. The variants are not silently combined.
- Alphonsus de Guimaraens, *Pastoral aos crentes do amor e da morte* (1923): eight complete Brazilian Portuguese works follow [Wikisource's scan-backed transcription](https://pt.wikisource.org/wiki/Pastoral_aos_crentes_do_amor_e_da_morte_(1923)) of the posthumous collection. The [USP catalogue](https://digital.bbm.usp.br/handle/bbm/7695) and [UFSC bibliography](https://literaturabrasileira.ufsc.br/documentos?id=142121) identify the 1923 printing; [Itaú Cultural](https://enciclopedia.itaucultural.org.br/pessoas/845-alphonsus-de-guimaraens) identifies the poet's 1870–1921 dates and Brazilian association. The historical poems and 1923 edition are public domain in the United States. Wikisource contributors' transcription is credited under [CC BY-SA 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/); consult linked article and transcluded scan-page histories and check local law. The [Commons scan](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Pastoral_aos_crentes_do_amor_e_da_morte_(1923).djvu) is marked public domain; USP's catalogue carries a default all-rights-reserved notice for its files unless otherwise indicated. We link the USP scan for inspection, not reproduce its images, and do not treat either institution's notice as a legal ruling.
  - The eight individual works are listed in the printed contents and were checked line by line against their 1923 scan pages. Four numbered works have no printed titles: the catalogue identifies them by their opening lines. Modern-spelling display titles, including *Ismália* and *A Catedral*, aid search; the reader also shows the historical titles *Ysmalia* and *A Cathedral*. The Faith theme for two works reflects their prayer or Christian imagery, not an assertion of the poet's denomination; religious imagery alone is not a claim that every poem is devotional. The exact composition dates and places are not independently established here. *A Cathedral* carries a separately printed date, July 14, 1914, which is disclosed but omitted from the verse.
  - In *Olhos*, the print reads `impassiveis`, correcting Wikisource's `impassoveis`. One space before an exclamation mark in *A Cathedral* is normalized. Four stanza breaks across page turns follow the surrounding quatrain or refrain pattern, as recorded in the [manifest](../scripts/sources/alphonsus-pastoral-reviewed.json); a page turn alone does not establish a stanza break. Punctuation, capitalization and indentation otherwise follow the reading transcription rather than a diplomatic facsimile. Wikisource marks these pages proofread, not independently validated. The 1899 *Setenário das Dores de Nossa Senhora* is a separate work: no text from it is included in this selection.
- Victor Hugo, *Les Contemplations*, books I–III: 55 individually numbered works are transcribed from the proofread [Wikisource Nelson edition (1911)](https://fr.wikisource.org/wiki/Les_Contemplations), with each article linked at its recorded revision and its contributors credited under [CC BY-SA 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/). Consult each linked article and its transcluded scan-page histories for contributors. The original collection was [published in 1856](https://essentiels.bnf.fr/fr/image/7c6f1314-f9eb-4525-9f2b-d99dc79e68ae-contemplations-4); the 1911 edition is public domain in the United States. Each selected verse was checked line by line against the independently transcribed [1858 edition, Gutenberg 29843](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/29843), normalizing case, accents, punctuation, ligatures and spaces. This comparison checks boundaries and words, not identical punctuation or identical editions; works with unresolved differences were excluded. For example, Gutenberg's I.IV has `aute` where the Wikisource text has `Saute`. The separately printed colophons, notes and page numbers are omitted; spaces are normalized and stanza breaks follow the transcribed edition, without adding breaks at scan-page boundaries. Untitled works use their first verse as a catalogue title. Hugo's printed dates are not independently treated as composition dates. No translations are supplied, and the pre-existing *Demain, dès l’aube* remains unchanged.
- Guillaume Apollinaire, *Alcools*: 34 complete, individually titled works follow the validated Wikisource transcription of the [third edition (1920)](https://fr.wikisource.org/wiki/Alcools), whose [scan is marked public domain](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Apollinaire_-_Alcools.djvu). The [BnF](https://catalogue.bnf.fr/ark:/12148/cb11888978p.public) identifies Apollinaire as a French poet (1880–1918), born in Rome and dead in Paris; France is an association with the poet, not a composition-place claim. [BnF](https://passerelles.essentiels.bnf.fr/fr/chronologie/article/d868b59a-74ab-47ba-8540-8904e405ca27-alcools-guillaume-apollinaire) dates the collection's first publication to 1913. The originals and 1920 edition are public domain in the United States. Apollinaire was declared *mort pour la France*; [Franceinfo](https://www.franceinfo.fr/culture/livres/apollinaire-dans-le-domaine-public-une-nouvelle-vie-pour-alcools_3356799.html) reports the expiry of his French economic rights in 2013 after wartime extensions. This is not legal advice, and local law and moral rights may differ. Wikisource transcriptions are credited to their contributors under [CC BY-SA 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/); consult the linked article and transcluded scan-page histories.
  - The selected verses were compared with the separate [Gutenberg 15462 transcription](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/15462), normalizing case, accents, punctuation and ligatures, while preserving word boundaries. Its source edition is not identified, so it is a comparison, not proof that editions or punctuation match. For *Zone*, Gutenberg wraps long verses, so the comparison is word-by-word rather than line-by-line. Substantive differences and uncertain stanza breaks were checked against the 1920 scans; a validated transcription can still contain errors. *Le Pont Mirabeau*'s [1920 scan](https://fr.wikisource.org/wiki/Page:Apollinaire_-_Alcools.djvu/17) includes a final two-line refrain omitted by Gutenberg. *Chantre* is a complete, separately titled [one-line poem](https://fr.wikisource.org/wiki/Page:Apollinaire_-_Alcools.djvu/39), not a fragment.
  - The 1920 *Zone* reads `en élevant`, `flamands`, `fonds` and `la regarder`, where Gutenberg differs. On [p. 14](https://fr.wikisource.org/wiki/Page:Apollinaire_-_Alcools.djvu/14), the print has `devant devant`; both transcriptions have only one `devant`, so the printed repetition is restored. The extractor retains long lines embedded outside normal verse paragraphs and joins six breaks contradicted by the print, including the transition at `l’avenue des Ternes`. In *Rhénane d’automne*, the 1920 print has `Plein des saules` and `La croix nous pousse`, while Gutenberg has `de saules` and `vous pousse`; the stanza break after `pieds` is restored from [p. 127](https://fr.wikisource.org/wiki/Page:Apollinaire_-_Alcools.djvu/127). The [*Les Colchiques* scan](https://fr.wikisource.org/wiki/Page:Apollinaire_-_Alcools.djvu/35) separates `Qui battent` as a one-line stanza, and [*1909*, p. 149](https://fr.wikisource.org/wiki/Page:Apollinaire_-_Alcools.djvu/149) separates its last seven lines into stanzas of three and four; Gutenberg does not.
  - Other printed readings retained include *Marie*'s `Des masques` versus `Les masques`, *Mai*'s `flétris` versus `fleuris`, *Rosemonde*'s `je m’allai` versus `je m’en allai`, and *Les Sapins*' `revêtus` versus `revêtu`. Further differences and image hashes are recorded in the [manifest](../scripts/sources/apollinaire-alcools-1920.json). *Marizibill* contains a historical antisemitic stereotype; *Schinderhannes* depicts a planned killing of a Jewish character. Separate dedications and printed dates are omitted; spaces and indentation are normalized, and accented capitals may follow the transcription rather than the typography of the scan. These are reading texts, not facsimiles. Composition dates, places, translations and the unreviewed remaining works are not asserted or supplied.
- Giacomo Leopardi, *Canti*: 32 distinct originals follow the proofread, scan-backed [Donati edition (Bari: Laterza, 1917)](https://it.wikisource.org/wiki/Canti_%28Leopardi_-_Donati%29), edited by [Alessandro Donati (1861–1934)](https://it.wikisource.org/wiki/Autore:Alessandro_Donati). The 1917 edition is public domain in the United States; the Wikisource transcription and contributors are credited under [CC BY-SA 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/), with each article linked at its recorded revision. Consult the linked article and transcluded scan-page histories for contributors. We compared all verse lines with the [1920 Hoepli edition in Gutenberg 55236](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/55236), and, where available, the separately transcribed [1835 Starita edition](https://it.wikisource.org/wiki/Canti_%281835%29), normalizing case, accents, punctuation, ligatures and spaces. For selected works I–XXXI and XXXVI, lines not corroborated by either comparison were checked against the 1917 scan; for XXXII and XXXIV, differences with the 1920 edition were checked against the scan because the earlier version differs substantially or does not include the work. This does not claim the editions or their punctuation are identical. Gutenberg has OCR errors, including `mortai` and `lo compagne` in *A Silvia*; comparison is not permission to copy such errors. The [manifest](../scripts/sources/leopardi-1917.json) records scan-page checks, hashes and the following explicit corrections:
  - *Il risorgimento*, line 41: the 1917 print has `quando`, but the 1835 and 1920 editions have `quanto`, which is used here. Line 143 reads `foco` in the [1917 scan](https://it.wikisource.org/wiki/Pagina:Canti_%28Leopardi_-_Donati%29.djvu/87), not the transcription's `loco`.
  - *Palinodia*, lines 7 and 38: the 1917 scans print lower-case [`meraviglia`](https://it.wikisource.org/wiki/Pagina:Canti_%28Leopardi_-_Donati%29.djvu/127) and [`Aureo`](https://it.wikisource.org/wiki/Pagina:Canti_%28Leopardi_-_Donati%29.djvu/128), rather than the transcription's `Meraviglia` and `Auro`.
  - *Il tramonto della luna*, line 63: the 1917 print has [`immortal`](https://it.wikisource.org/wiki/Pagina:Canti_%28Leopardi_-_Donati%29.djvu/138); we use `mortal`, as in the [annotated Starita copy and its editorial note](https://it.wikisource.org/w/index.php?title=Canti_%28Starita_corretta_1835%29/Il_tramonto_della_luna&oldid=3898400) and the 1920 edition. The annotated-copy transcription has not had all pages independently reread. Its note says lines 63–68 are in Ranieri's hand and that the deathbed-dictation story is disputed; it does not establish that he authored them.
  - *La ginestra*, lines 75 and 169: the 1917 scans print [`dalla`](https://it.wikisource.org/wiki/Pagina:Canti_%28Leopardi_-_Donati%29.djvu/141) and [`immense in guisa`](https://it.wikisource.org/wiki/Pagina:Canti_%28Leopardi_-_Donati%29.djvu/144) without a comma. The complete 317-line Donati version omits three lines after line 64 which appear in the 1920 edition; these versions are not silently combined.

  The separately printed epigraphs in *Amore e Morte*, *Palinodia* and *La ginestra* are omitted and identified in their reader contexts. Line numbers, notes, page markers and indentation are omitted; *Il primo amore*'s indented tercets are represented by stanza breaks. No translations are supplied. The three existing 1826 idylls remain unchanged, and later versions of those same works are not counted again.
- Petrarch, *Le rime di M. Francesco Petrarca* (Venice: Giuseppe Bortoli, 1739): 59 complete sonnets follow the scan-backed [Italian Wikisource transcription](https://it.wikisource.org/wiki/Le_rime_di_M._Francesco_Petrarca), credited to its contributors under [CC BY-SA 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/); consult the linked article and transcluded scan-page histories. [Commons](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Le_rime_di_M._Francesco_Petrarca_I.djvu) marks the historical scan public domain, and the [Internet Archive copy](https://archive.org/details/ScansionePIII200MiscellaneaOpal), digitized by the University of Turin's OPAL project, is marked CC0. The original poems and 1739 edition are public domain in the United States; local law and moral rights may differ. [Treccani](https://www.treccani.it/enciclopedia/francesco-petrarca/) identifies Petrarca (1304–1374) and his Italian associations; Italy is a poet association, not a claim about a poem's place of composition.
  - The 1739 edition numbers sonnets and canzoni **separately**. Our `Sonetto` numbers are those of that printing, not modern *Canzoniere* / *Rerum vulgarium fragmenta* numbers. Each poem is bounded by its own printed sonnet heading and the next heading, and its 14 lines were checked against the 1739 scan. The validated Wikisource transcription is not infallible: the [manifest](../scripts/sources/petrarch-rime-1739.json) records all scan-based edits, article revisions, image hashes and output hashes. For instance, the scans read [`Per far`](https://archive.org/details/ScansionePIII200MiscellaneaOpal/page/n85/mode/1up), not `Per fare`; [`dal vero`](https://archive.org/details/ScansionePIII200MiscellaneaOpal/page/n89/mode/1up), not `del vero`; and [`Poco`](https://archive.org/details/ScansionePIII200MiscellaneaOpal/page/n124/mode/1up), not `Poro`. Each affected reader page also identifies its edits.
  - The four 1739 sonnets XXVIII, LXIX, XCVIII and CXXVI were compared line by line with separately transcribed selections from [St John Lucas's *Oxford Book of Italian Verse* (1910)](https://it.wikisource.org/wiki/The_Oxford_book_of_Italian_verse), with article links and normalized differences recorded in the manifest. These are different historical editions, not proof of identical wording or punctuation. For *Solo, e pensoso*, the 1739 print reads `fuggir` and `Dove ... la rena`, while Oxford reads `fuggire` and `Ove ... l’arena`; for *Erano i capei*, it has `all’aura` and `maraviglia`, versus `a l’aura` and `meraviglia`. For *Quel vago impallidir*, the scan confirms `pensier`, not the 1739 Wikisource transcription's `penser`; the editions also differ at `siccome` / `sì come` and `fosse` / `fusse`. For *In qual parte*, the scan has `esempio`, not that transcription's `essempio`.
  - Sonnets I and VII were additionally compared with the Italian in [Gutenberg 50307](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/50307), a transcription of a 1903 edition. Its transcriber consulted Contini's 1964 edition when correcting other selections; we use neither the English translations nor that modern edition as a source of our text. The widely available [other Wikisource *Canzoniere* transcription](https://it.wikisource.org/wiki/Discussione:Canzoniere_(Rerum_vulgarium_fragmenta)) explicitly identifies Contini's 1964 critical text and is not used for the import. Comparisons normalize case, accents and punctuation while preserving word boundaries, so they do not prove matching punctuation. The 1739 drop capitals and small capitals are converted to ordinary text; spaces are normalized, and editorial stanza breaks represent the printed indentation. Punctuation and some accents may follow Wikisource rather than the print: this is a reading transcription, not a facsimile. Exact composition dates and places and translations are not supplied; the explicitly prayerful *Padre del Ciel* alone is tagged Faith, without inferring the poet's denomination.
- Francis of Assisi, *Laudes Creaturarum*: the original follows the scan-backed [Wikisource transcription, revision 3719546](https://it.wikisource.org/w/index.php?title=Laudes_creaturarum_(Lucas)&oldid=3719546), of St John Lucas's [*Oxford Book of Italian Verse* (1910), pp. 37–38](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:The_Oxford_book_of_Italian_verse.djvu). The pre-1931 edition is public domain in the United States; the Wikisource transcription is credited to its contributors under [CC BY-SA 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/). Consult article and transcluded scan-page histories, and check local law. The edition identifies the author as San Francesco d'Assisi and gives c. 1182–1226; Italy is an association with the poet, not an asserted composition location. The poem is complete through `Amen.` on [p. 38](https://it.wikisource.org/wiki/Pagina:The_Oxford_book_of_Italian_verse.djvu/38). On [p. 37](https://it.wikisource.org/wiki/Pagina:The_Oxford_book_of_Italian_verse.djvu/37), the first line is present in Wikisource as a drop capital outside the usual verse paragraph; the import handles it explicitly, rendering the print's small capitals as `Altissimu`. The scan has `La quale è`, rather than the transcription's `La quale e`.
  - The separate [Codex 338 transcription, revision 3676436](https://it.wikisource.org/w/index.php?title=Cantico_di_Frate_Sole&oldid=3676436), is a comparison, not the source of the 1910 text. It reads `lo qual è iorno et allumini noi per loi`, whereas the 1910 print reads `Lo quale lu jorno allumeni per nui`; other readings also differ. The two are not silently combined. The manuscript reproduction has separate image rights; no image or modern translation is imported.
- Five laude by Jacopone da Todi follow the proofread Wikisource transcription of Giovanni Ferri's [*Le Laude* (1915)](https://it.wikisource.org/wiki/Le_Laude_(1915)), which reproduces the 1490 princeps. The early edition is public domain in the United States; Wikisource contributors are credited under [CC BY-SA 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/). Consult each linked article and scan-page history and check local law. [Franca Ageno Brambilla's scholarly biography](https://www.treccani.it/enciclopedia/iacopo-benedetti_(Dizionario-Biografico)/) identifies laude XVIII, XXVIII, XXXII and XXXIII by incipit and explains that the *first* of its paired numbers is the princeps/Ferri number; [Treccani's literary history](https://www.treccani.it/enciclopedia/iacopone-da-todi-e-la-poesia-religiosa_(Storia-della-civilt%C3%A0-europea-a-cura-di-Umberto-Eco)) identifies *Donna de Paradiso* (Ferri XCIII). The biography discusses Jacopone's Todi association and uncertain birth and death details; Italy is an association with the poet, not a composition-location claim. Other contested or insufficiently corroborated entries and *Stabat Mater* are excluded.
  - Each selected poem was checked against its complete 1915 scan and compared line by line with [Gutenberg 29977](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/29977), a separate transcription of the **same** edition, not an independent historical witness. The comparison retains accents and punctuation, normalizing whitespace, apostrophe shape and spaces around dashes. The scans resolve transcription errors including `einbrigato` → `embrigato`, `piacenza` → `placenza`, `ventate` → `veritate`, punctuation in XXXIII, and `está` → `esta` in XCIII. In [XCIII, p. 232](https://it.wikisource.org/wiki/Pagina:Iacopone_da_Todi_%E2%80%93_Le_Laude,_1915_%E2%80%93_BEIC_1853668.djvu/238), the scan prints `hatte`, not the Wikisource transcription's `batte`; the 1915 reading is retained without asserting that other editions agree. On [XXXII, p. 65](https://it.wikisource.org/wiki/Pagina:Iacopone_da_Todi_%E2%80%93_Le_Laude,_1915_%E2%80%93_BEIC_1853668.djvu/71), the print has `vol` where Gutenberg has `voi`. The [manifest](../scripts/sources/francis-jacopone-reviewed.json) pins the reviewed texts, corrections, differences and scan-image hashes.
  - For both authors, spaces around dashes are normalized and printed indentation is represented by stanza breaks; page numbers and footnotes are omitted. The result is a reading transcription, not a diplomatic facsimile, and small typographic accents can be difficult to resolve in the scan. *Donna del paradiso* and *Canticle of the Creatures* are discovery titles, not translations. No translations or exact composition dates or places are supplied; not every lauda is classified as devotional solely because of its author.
- [*Poems by Emily Dickinson*, Gutenberg 12242](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/12242): the first (1890) and second (1891) series, edited by Mabel Loomis Todd and T. W. Higginson. Their assigned titles and altered punctuation are not a diplomatic transcription of Dickinson's manuscripts. The poems are credited to the early published editions, not to modern editors.
- [*Les Fleurs du Mal*, Gutenberg 6099](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/6099): a limited set of separately headed French originals by Charles Baudelaire. The eBook does not identify its particular print edition and contains possible transcription errors; other headings that combine poems were not imported.
- Baudelaire, *Les Fleurs du mal*: 48 additional, distinct French works follow the validated Wikisource transcription of the [1861 second edition](https://fr.wikisource.org/wiki/Les_Fleurs_du_mal_(1861)). The [scanned title page](https://fr.wikisource.org/wiki/Page:Baudelaire_-_Les_Fleurs_du_mal_1861.djvu/6) identifies the edition and year; the original poems and 1861 edition are public domain in the United States. Wikisource contributors are credited under [CC BY-SA 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/); consult the article and transcluded scan-page histories. The [manifest](../scripts/sources/baudelaire-fleurs-1861.json) records each article revision, the 100 page revisions used, and extracted-text hashes. Each verse is found in order in the recorded page revisions and compared line by line with the separate [Apollinaire-edited 1924 transcription, Gutenberg 61565](https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/61565). That volume combines material from several editions and includes variants: it is a comparison, not the source of the 1861 reading or proof of independent textual descent. Comparison ignores case, accents, ligatures, punctuation **and all spaces**, so it does not establish identical spelling, word breaks or punctuation. Proofreading status and matching digital transcriptions do not guarantee a perfect scan reading.
  - The [1861 contents](https://fr.wikisource.org/wiki/Les_Fleurs_du_mal_(1861)) identify each numbered work. Numbers XXIV, XXVII and XXXIX have no printed titles and are identified here by their opening lines; two distinct works called *Spleen* retain their own openings. The 32 selections with two quatrains followed by two tercets are classified as sonnets for discovery; this is a form description, not an assertion that every sonnet in the volume is included. The pre-existing 14 Baudelaire records, including their URLs and separately sourced translations, are unchanged; this is a selection, not a complete 1861 edition. Latin *Franciscæ meæ Laudes*, multipart works and other works not yet corroborated here are deferred, not counted as French originals or fragments. Differences also require separate review: for example, the 1861 Wikisource text of *La Muse malade* has `réfléchis` where 1924 has `s’étaler`, and its *Le Soleil* has `traits` where 1924 has `coups`. These examples are excluded rather than silently blended or treated as proof that either transcription is error-free.
  - Dedications, epigraphs, page numbers and indentation are omitted; spaces and some typography follow the reading transcription, not a diplomatic facsimile. Stanza spacing follows that transcription without automatically adding a break at a page turn. The 1924 comparison divides portions of *L’Invitation au voyage*, *Paysage* and *Le Crépuscule du soir* differently; the [1861 scan of *L’Invitation*](https://fr.wikisource.org/wiki/Page:Baudelaire_-_Les_Fleurs_du_mal_1861.djvu/131) supports the retained 12-line stanza, while breaks at other page transitions may be ambiguous. These differences are disclosed on the affected readers. No composition dates, composition places or new translations are asserted.
- Four English renderings of Baudelaire are separately attributed to F. P. Sturm and transcribed from [*Baudelaire: His Prose and Poetry*, edited by T. R. Smith, New York, 1919](https://www.gutenberg.org/files/47032/47032-h/47032-h.htm#THE_FLOWERS_OF_EVIL). The contents of that edition identify Sturm as the translator; the translation rights note and source appear independently of each French original.

Gutenberg identifies these eBooks as public domain in the United States. Rights and moral rights elsewhere may differ, especially for later transcriptions and editions; check local law. Apart from the separately identified Sturm translations, the imports do not supply translations or historical influence claims. The historical source title for the 1922 anthology is retained for accurate citation.

For reproducibility, download the following Gutenberg files under the listed local names:

| Local filename | Source |
| --- | --- |
| `tang-52323.txt` | [eBook 52323 text](https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/52323/pg52323.txt) |
| `spanish-100.html` | [eBook 65880 HTML](https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/65880/pg65880-images.html) |
| `black-american-verse.html` | [eBook 11986 HTML](https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/11986/pg11986-images.html) |
| `espanca.html` | [eBook 17610 HTML](https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/17610/pg17610-images.html) |
| `fleurs.html` | [eBook 6099 HTML](https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/6099/pg6099-images.html) |
| `emily-source.txt` | [eBook 12242 text](https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/12242/pg12242.txt) |

Run `python3 scripts/import-tang.py /path/to/tang-52323.txt` and `uv run --no-project --with beautifulsoup4 python scripts/import-anthologies.py /path/to/sources`. The scripts verify SHA-256 checksums before extracting and require review if the upstream files change. They extract works, not Gutenberg's license boilerplate. The committed JSON makes builds independent of network access.

To reproduce the Hugo selection, save the [1858 HTML](https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/29843/pg29843-images.html) as `hugo-1858.html`, and save each article revision listed in [`scripts/sources/hugo-1911.json`](../scripts/sources/hugo-1911.json) as `BOOK-NUMBER.html` (for example, `1-01.html`) in the same directory. Run `uv run --no-project --with beautifulsoup4 python scripts/import-hugo.py /path/to/sources --check`. The manifest pins each extracted verse text and article revision; Wikisource articles transclude scan pages, so an article revision alone does not freeze later changes to those pages. Any text or comparison-source drift requires review before regeneration.

To reproduce the Apollinaire selection, save [Gutenberg 15462 HTML](https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/15462/pg15462-images.html) as `gutenberg.html`; save each `source` revision in the [manifest](../scripts/sources/apollinaire-alcools-1920.json) with the legacy parser (`&useparsoid=0`) as `NN.html`, zero-padding its selection number (for example, `01.html`). For every scan number `N` in `scanSha256`, save `https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c6/Apollinaire_-_Alcools.djvu/pageN-1280px-Apollinaire_-_Alcools.djvu.jpg` as `scan-N.jpg`. Run `uv run --no-project --with beautifulsoup4 python scripts/import-apollinaire-alcools.py /path/to/sources --check`. The script checks scan hashes, source revisions, validated status, transcluded text, comparison differences and output hashes. An article revision does not freeze transcluded pages; scan-to-text and boundary review remain manual, not automated proof.

To reproduce the Baudelaire selection, run `uv run --no-project --with beautifulsoup4 python scripts/import-baudelaire-1861.py /path/to/sources --fetch --check`. Fetch retrieves missing article revisions, the recorded page-revision contents, Gutenberg comparison and selected scan images; it never overwrites existing source files. The script checks their pinned hashes and revision identifiers, validated status, ordered verse lines and comparison text before comparing the generated records. Wikisource article revisions alone do not freeze transclusions; a changed reading, comparison or scan requires review rather than silently updating the manifest. The scan-page text check is normalized and is not a visual, line-by-line collation.

To reproduce the Leopardi selection, save the [1920 Gutenberg HTML](https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/55236/pg55236-images.html) as `hoepli-1920.html`. For each entry in [`scripts/sources/leopardi-1917.json`](../scripts/sources/leopardi-1917.json), save the `source` article revision as `donati-NN.html` and, except for no. 34, its `olderSource` revision as `starita-NN.html`; no. 33 uses the annotated Starita copy rather than the 1835 printed edition. The saved pages must use the legacy parser (`&useparsoid=0`). Run `uv run --no-project --with beautifulsoup4 python scripts/import-leopardi.py /path/to/sources --check`. The manifest pins article revisions, extracted texts, comparison results and scan-reviewed line numbers; article revisions alone do not freeze transcluded scan pages. Any drift requires review before regeneration.

To reproduce Petrarch, save each `source` revision in the [manifest](../scripts/sources/petrarch-rime-1739.json) with Wikisource's legacy parser (`&useparsoid=0`) as `sonnet-NNN.html`, zero-padding the 1739 sonnet number. Save each Oxford `comparison.url` revision as its `comparison.filename`, and [Gutenberg 50307 HTML](https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/50307/pg50307-images.html) as `gutenberg-1903.html`. Download the [Internet Archive JP2 zip](https://archive.org/download/ScansionePIII200MiscellaneaOpal/ScansionePIII200MiscellaneaOpal_jp2.zip) and, for every manifest `scan-NNN.jp2`, extract its `ScansionePIII200MiscellaneaOpal_jp2/ScansionePIII200MiscellaneaOpal_XXXX.jp2` member, where `XXXX` is `NNN − 1` padded to four digits. Run `uv run --no-project --with beautifulsoup4 python scripts/import-petrarch-rime.py /path/to/sources --check`. It verifies scan hashes, revision and proofreading status, complete extracted texts, corrections, available comparisons and output hashes. Article revisions do not freeze transcluded scan pages; manual image-to-text and boundary review is not automated proof. The Internet Archive and Wikisource scans derive from the same historical edition, not independent witnesses.

To reproduce the Machado selection, save the [1901 Gutenberg HTML](https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/61653/pg61653-images.html) as `gutenberg-1901.html`. For each entry in [`scripts/sources/machado-reviewed.json`](../scripts/sources/machado-reviewed.json), save its `source` article revision using the legacy parser (`&useparsoid=0`) as `SLUG.html`, and save the two `comparisonUrl` pages as `citador-fe.html` and `citador-caridade.html`. Run `uv run --no-project --with beautifulsoup4 python scripts/import-machado.py /path/to/sources --check`. The manifest pins extracted text, revisions and comparison results; Wikisource article revisions do not freeze transcluded scan pages. Any source drift requires review before regeneration.

To reproduce the Auta selection, save every `source` revision in the [manifest](../scripts/sources/auta-horto-reviewed.json) with Wikisource's legacy parser (`&useparsoid=0`) as `SLUG.html`; save each listed `comparison` page as `comparison-SLUG.html`. For each scan page `N` in `scanSha256`, save `https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/Horto_%281910%29.djvu/pageN-1280px-Horto_%281910%29.djvu.jpg` as `horto-N.jpg`. Run `uv run --no-project --with beautifulsoup4 python scripts/import-auta-horto.py /path/to/sources --check`. The script checks image hashes, article revision, proofreading status, transcluded text and expected line differences; an article revision does not freeze its transcluded pages. Image-to-text and poem-boundary verification remain manual, not automated proof.

To reproduce Francisca Júlia, save the [Commons PDF](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/e/e8/Marmores.pdf) as `Marmores.pdf`; for each `source` revision in the [manifest](../scripts/sources/francisca-julia-marmores-reviewed.json), save the legacy-parser HTML (`&useparsoid=0`) as `article-NN.html`, zero-padding `articleNumber` (for example, `article-01.html` and `article-36.html`). Run `uv run --no-project --with beautifulsoup4 python scripts/import-francisca-julia-marmores.py /path/to/sources --check`. The importer checks the PDF hash, article revision, proofreading status, transcluded pages and text, corrections, stanza breaks, and output hashes. An article revision does not freeze transcluded pages. Scan-to-text and boundary checks remain manual, not automated proof; changed sources require review.

To reproduce Alphonsus de Guimaraens, save the [USP PDF](https://digital.bbm.usp.br/bitstream/bbm/7695/1/45000023470_Output.o.pdf) as `Pastoral-USP.pdf` and each `source` revision in the [manifest](../scripts/sources/alphonsus-pastoral-reviewed.json) as `article-NN.html`, adding `&useparsoid=0` and zero-padding `articleNumber`. Run `uv run --no-project --with beautifulsoup4 python scripts/import-alphonsus-pastoral.py /path/to/sources --check`. The importer checks the scan hash, article revision, proofreading status, transcluded page range and text, corrections, restored breaks, the omitted date and output hashes. Article revisions do not freeze transcluded pages. Scan-to-text and boundary verification remain manual; changed sources require review.

To reproduce the Bashō selection, save each distinct `source` scan-page revision in [`scripts/sources/basho-oku-reviewed.json`](../scripts/sources/basho-oku-reviewed.json) as `source-page-N.html`, the [1911 travelogue revision](https://ja.wikisource.org/w/index.php?title=%E3%81%8A%E3%81%8F%E3%81%AE%E3%81%BB%E3%81%9D%E9%81%93&oldid=247172&useparsoid=0) as `oku-1911.html`, and the [association's 50-verse list](https://www.basho-bp.jp/?page_id=28) as `basho-association.html`. Save Wikisource pages with the legacy parser (`&useparsoid=0`). Run `uv run --no-project --with beautifulsoup4 python scripts/import-basho-oku.py /path/to/sources --check`. The association's individual comparison strings are hash-pinned; later changes require review.

To reproduce the *Nozarashi* selection, save each distinct `source` scan-page revision in [`scripts/sources/basho-nozarashi-reviewed.json`](../scripts/sources/basho-nozarashi-reviewed.json) as `noz-page-N.html`, the [1929 travelogue transcription](https://ja.wikisource.org/w/index.php?title=%E9%87%8E%E3%81%96%E3%82%89%E3%81%97%E7%B4%80%E8%A1%8C&oldid=179298&useparsoid=0) as `nozarashi.html`, and the [association's 43-verse list](https://www.basho-bp.jp/?page_id=20) as `assoc-nozarashi.html`. Save Wikisource pages with the legacy parser (`&useparsoid=0`), then run `uv run --no-project --with beautifulsoup4 python scripts/import-basho-nozarashi.py /path/to/sources --check`. The association's comparison strings are hash-pinned; changes require review.

To reproduce the Buson selection, save the [Kigosai comparison page](https://kigosai.sub.jp/bs/?page_id=25582) as `buson-kigosai.html`. For each scan number `N` in the [manifest](../scripts/sources/buson-1926-reviewed.json), save `https://dl.ndl.go.jp/api/iiif/1019573/R00000NN/full/2400,/0/default.jpg` as `buson-1926-NN.jpg`, zero-padding the scan number to two digits (for example, `R0000009` and `buson-1926-09.jpg`). Run `uv run --no-project --with beautifulsoup4 python scripts/import-buson-1926.py /path/to/sources --check`. The script checks the saved image hashes and the individual comparison strings before regenerating the records; the image-to-text transcription itself remains a manual review, not OCR or automated proof.

To reproduce the al-Mutanabbī selection, save the [1890–91 volume 1 PDF](https://odyssee.univ-amu.fr/files/original/1/15/Mutanabbi-Vol1.pdf) as `mutanabbi-1890.pdf` and each distinct [al-Wāḥidī page](https://shamela.ws/book/754) identified by `comparisonPage` in the [manifest](../scripts/sources/mutanabbi-1890-reviewed.json) as `wahidi-NNN.html` (for example, `wahidi-003.html`). Run `uv run --no-project --with beautifulsoup4 python scripts/import-mutanabbi-1890.py /path/to/sources --check`. It verifies the full PDF hash, all selected comparison half-lines and the explicitly recorded differences. Scan transcription and boundary review remain manual, not automated proof; a changed PDF or comparison requires review.

To reproduce Abū Firās, save the [1895 Dvořák PDF](https://archive.org/download/abfirseinara00abfi/abfirseinara00abfi.pdf) as `dvorak-1895.pdf`, the [1910 *Dīwān* PDF](https://archive.org/download/diwanabifiras00abufuoft/diwanabifiras00abufuoft.pdf) as `abu-firas-1910.pdf`, and the [1859 Ahlwardt PDF](https://archive.org/download/chalefelahmarsq00ahlwgoog/chalefelahmarsq00ahlwgoog.pdf) as `ahlwardt-1859.pdf`. Run `uv run --no-project python scripts/import-abu-firas-1895.py /path/to/sources --check`. The script checks all three PDF hashes, manifest shape, line counts and regenerated records; it does **not** automatically read the scans or prove the transcriptions, boundaries or comparisons. Those remain manual checks, and changed source PDFs require review.

To reproduce the Francis/Jacopone selection, save the [Gutenberg 29977 HTML](https://www.gutenberg.org/files/29977/29977-h/29977-h.htm) as `giacopone-ferri-1915-gutenberg.html`. Save the Francis `source` and `comparison` revisions in the [manifest](../scripts/sources/francis-jacopone-reviewed.json) as `francis-lucas-1910.html` and `francis-codex-338.html`; save each Jacopone `source` revision as `ferri-NN.html`. Use Wikisource's legacy parser (`&useparsoid=0`). For each `francis-scan-N.jpg` in the manifest, save `https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4a/The_Oxford_book_of_Italian_verse.djvu/pageN-1280px-The_Oxford_book_of_Italian_verse.djvu.jpg`. For each `ferri-scan-N.jpg`, save `https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c8/Iacopone_da_Todi_%E2%80%93_Le_Laude%2C_1915_%E2%80%93_BEIC_1853668.djvu/pageN-1920px-Iacopone_da_Todi_%E2%80%93_Le_Laude%2C_1915_%E2%80%93_BEIC_1853668.djvu.jpg`. Run `uv run --no-project --with beautifulsoup4 python scripts/import-francis-jacopone.py /path/to/sources --check`. Article revisions alone do not freeze transcluded scan pages; the script also checks extracted texts, comparison differences and image hashes. Scan-to-text and poem-boundary review remain manual, not automated proof.

## Editorial standard

Keep author attribution, original text, edition, translator, dates, source, rights and historical evidence distinct. Use unknown or qualified values when facts are uncertain. Documented influence requires a citation; a shared theme or adjacent publication is not influence. Obtain permission before adding protected modern poems or translations. Never bulk-import texts merely to reach a target count.

## Discovery classification checklist

- Use neutral literary, language, time or geographic descriptions rather than categories based on sensitive personal identity. Do not infer sensitive traits from a name, language, anthology or poem.
- Make each category's label match its actual scope: a broad place category should include all matching recorded associations, while a single anthology should be identified as a selection. An associated place is not necessarily a nationality or the place of composition.
- Support new place associations with evidence recorded alongside the record or in this document; preserve uncertainty and use “Not established” when evidence is insufficient. An anthology's title or publication location alone does not establish every author's nationality. Existing anthology importers contain broad geographic defaults that require individual source review; do not treat those defaults as evidence for new classifications.
- Keep original texts, historical edition titles, citations and rights notes accurate, even if a historical title contains language unsuitable for a discovery label. Use a neutral display label without rewriting source records, existing filter values or URLs.
- Before publishing, check labels and counts against all matching works, including multiple place associations, and verify that source attribution and JavaScript-free reading remain intact.
